---
name: llp-orient
description: Before editing a subsystem, file, or starting a task, assemble the governing LLP context — read the root LLP, follow the @ref annotations on the code in scope, and find the LLPs covering the relevant systems. Produces a compact context pack of the constraints a change must respect. Read-only.
source: ccheever/llp@v0.5.2
---

# llp-orient

<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0008-distributed-agent-skills.rfc.md#the-skills--core-five-plus-flags — skill contract -->
<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0000-linked-literate-programming.explainer.md#2-reference-syntax — the grammar this reads -->

Load the design rationale governing the area you're about to touch, before touching it. This is the consumption side of LLP — orienting first is what turns `@ref` from a possibility into a habit.

Invoke as `/llp-orient <path-or-task>` (file, directory, subsystem, or task description). With no argument, use the current change scope (`git diff --name-only`).

## Trigger

Start of a coding task that touches an area with documented design. The orient-first *rule* lives in the repo's always-loaded `AGENTS.md`; this skill is the detailed form that rule routes to for non-trivial work — don't rely on ambient description-match alone.

## Invariants

- MUST be read-only: never edit code or LLP documents.
- MUST scope to the task: pull the constraints that bind this change, not a corpus summary.
- MUST cite, not restate: reference policy as `LLP NNNN#anchor` rather than paraphrasing sections.
- MUST NOT invent constraints. If something isn't documented, say so.
- MUST NOT guess the rationale behind a broken `@ref` — report it and hand off to `llp-maintain`.

## Workflow

> **Recipe (advisory)** — corpus root = nearest `llp/` walking up from the path in scope (LLP 0001 §Unit of adoption); "no corpus" is an empty walk, not cwd-relative absence. Read `llp/foundation/` first (the kernel; the root LLP is there once `Active` — on a fresh adoption it is still `Draft` and sits in `current/`), then skim `llp/current/` (the declared working set); both hold relative links — symlinks, or small files containing the relative path — so `ls` answers and either form resolves. Then the root LLP's systems vocabulary. `Grep '@ref'` over the files in scope and `Read` each target — these are the highest-signal context. `Grep` the `**Systems:**` headers across `llp/` for LLPs governing the subsystems touched; read the governing ones (Spec, Decision, Principles, relevant RFCs). Follow `Related:` and nearby `@ref` targets one hop out; stop there unless something is clearly load-bearing. Stale-pin nudge (skip offline): read the installed tag from the skills receipt (or a `source:` pin), compare `git ls-remote --tags <origin>` — if upstream is newer, say so in one line ("skills at v0.3.0, upstream v0.5.2 — run /llp-adopt update") and move on; never update from here.

## Artifact

A context pack with these sections, faster to read than the documents it points at:

- **Scope** — files / subsystems queried
- **Governing LLPs** — numbered list, one-line gloss each; mark which are in `foundation/` and which are in `current/` (in-flight — its guidance may still move)
- **Active constraints** — bullets, each citing `LLP NNNN#anchor`
- **Relevant rationale** — the *why*, same citation form
- **Open questions / risks** for this area
- **Likely doc updates** if the change lands
- **Hand-off** — one of `none | llp-adopt | llp-create | llp-review | llp-maintain`

## Hand-offs

- No corpus (no `llp/` walking up from the path in scope) → `llp-adopt`.
- The task needs a decision no LLP covers → `llp-create` (or `llp-review` if proposal-shaped).
- An `@ref` target or anchor doesn't resolve → `llp-maintain`.

//! Bounded reading sections; the compiled corpus retains complete examples.
// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#9 — explicit reference reads use the same byte budget.
use super::{FULL_BYTES, reading_blocks};

/// Flatten nested reference headings and split at complete paragraphs/fences.
/// Long runnable references stay in the package, behind a file pointer.
pub fn reader_card(name: &str, card: &str) -> String {
    if card.len() <= FULL_BYTES { return card.into(); }
    let title = card.lines().next().unwrap_or("# Guide");
    let room = FULL_BYTES.saturating_sub(title.len() + 350);
    let mut out = format!("{title}\n\nRead one section for your change. `guide {name} --full` lists sections; --repeat reprints a selection.\n\n");
    let mut heading = "Opening".to_owned();
    let mut page = String::new();
    let mut part = 1;
    let flush = |out: &mut String, page: &mut String, heading: &str, part: usize| {
        if page.is_empty() { return; }
        out.push_str(&format!("## {heading}{}\n\n{}\n", if part == 1 { String::new() } else { format!(" (continued {part})") }, page));
        page.clear();
    };
    let mut fenced = false;
    // Isolate headings even where source prose has no preceding blank line.
    let mut prepared = String::new();
    for line in card.lines().skip(1) {
        if line.starts_with("```") {
            if !fenced { prepared.push('\n'); }
            fenced = !fenced;
        }
        if !fenced && (line.starts_with("## ") || line.starts_with("### ")) { prepared.push('\n'); }
        prepared.push_str(line); prepared.push('\n');
        if !fenced && (line.starts_with("## ") || line.starts_with("### ") || line.starts_with("```")) { prepared.push('\n'); }
    }
    for block in reading_blocks(&prepared) {
        if block.starts_with("## ") || block.starts_with("### ") {
            flush(&mut out, &mut page, &heading, part);
            heading = block.trim().trim_start_matches('#').trim().to_owned(); part = 1;
            continue;
        }
        let block = if block.len() > room && block.starts_with("```") {
            // The account-cycle example is the only oversized indivisible fence.
            format!("Complete runnable reference: node_modules/snapback4/dist/recipes/day2-accounts.mjs. Run it with Node; read it only when adapting that account-cycle example.\n")
        } else { block };
        // Dense prose can exceed a section; split only prose at word boundaries.
        let chunks = if block.len() > room {
            let mut chunks = Vec::new(); let mut chunk = String::new();
            for word in block.split_inclusive(char::is_whitespace) {
                if chunk.len() + word.len() > room && !chunk.is_empty() { chunks.push(std::mem::take(&mut chunk)); }
                chunk.push_str(word);
            }
            if !chunk.is_empty() { chunks.push(chunk); } chunks
        } else { vec![block] };
        for chunk in chunks {
            if page.len() + chunk.len() + 1 > room { flush(&mut out, &mut page, &heading, part); part += 1; }
            page.push_str(&chunk); page.push('\n');
        }
    }
    flush(&mut out, &mut page, &heading, part);
    out
}
# Day two: change, prove, release, return

Start here with an existing app. Keep its store and saved sources. Before editing:

```sh
npx snapback4 init --adapt --day2 --apply
npx snapback4 deploy ./deploy --save-version versions/v1
```

Adapt saves the current sources/fixtures in versions/v1 and versions/v2 if absent,
and a frozen v1/ client with its own ESM package and generated API. Existing
snapshots and application-owned proofs stay untouched. Run this before the v2
edit; a snapshot taken afterwards is not historical evidence. If v1 is already
saved/deployed, inspect `releases ./deploy --json`; reuse that saved source.

Read only this card, ONE change route below, and `guide api proof` when writing
proofs. Do not read deploy/production/evolution --full next: those commands list
reference sections. Do not read proof.js or the full declarations. Use the
signature digest and a specific section only when a refusal needs it.

## Choose one change route

| Change | Read at most these sections |
|---|---|
| Rename a field/table/operation or argument | `guide rename --section opening`; `guide deploy --section 'Keep v1 clients working'` |
| Loosen a required field, allow null or an empty album | `guide deploy --section 'Bridge a safety gate'`; `guide security --section 'N_PRESENCE_LOOSENING'` if the check asks for presence intent |
| Add a required field computed from old values | `guide deploy --section 'Migrations and adoption'`; `guide deploy --section 'Which migration shape'` |
| Change record kind, move media into children, split a list | `guide evolution --section 'Move media'`; `guide deploy --section 'Keep v1 clients working'` |

For a spelling change use `renamed from`; retain old endpoint/argument aliases
and result wrappers for v1 callers. Backfill computes historical values; it
isn't maintenance on later edits. Guard every creating/updating path that can
change a derived field. New required columns need a literal default or named
backfill; `default now`/`viewer` cannot invent history. Media/reference moves
need the supported extract shape, not a scalar transform. A loosened type does
not authorize an absent value at every workflow transition: keep independent
presence intent and current guards. Answer the actual check question; a
presence tautology is no safety proof.

## Preview, deploy v2, rollback and forward

Update your working snapback/*.q and versions/v2 (source and contemporary
fixtures); keep versions/v1 frozen. With production v1 already in ./deploy:

```sh
npx snapback4 check
npx snapback4 test --source versions/v2
npx snapback4 deploy ./deploy --schema versions/v2 --check
npx snapback4 deploy ./deploy --schema versions/v2
npx snapback4 rollback ./deploy --to 1 --check
npx snapback4 rollback ./deploy --to 1
npx snapback4 rollback ./deploy --to latest --check
npx snapback4 rollback ./deploy --to latest
```

Replace 1 with the saved v1 release ID. Preview changes no live state and later
writes can change its result. Await activation; no sleep is needed. Default
rollback may use a registered compatible view retaining current guards. Inspect
`releases ./deploy --json` for live source/view and row shape; a successful
rollback alone does not establish frozen-v1 result compatibility. Use --exact
only after deliberately reviewing the old source's broader policy. If preview
requires a bridge or --restore-dormant, follow its concrete instruction and
preview again. Never prune/reset/delete storage to make a proof pass.

Run `npm run day2:proof` twice. The generated proof owns a fresh temporary store,
checks persisted IDs/values across two rollback/forward cycles and cleans up.
Its default business hook proves storage conservation only: add authenticated
v1/v2 business calls, refusals and reads of v2-native rows. Return explicit
changed-field/deletion declarations for intentional old-client writes. A custom
proof must also preserve data and stop every host it starts.

## Frozen v1 calls and repeatable users

```sh
BACKEND_URL=http://127.0.0.1:4400 CALLS_USERS='{"member":[{"email":"...","password":"..."}]}' npm --prefix v1 run client:calls
```

The frozen runner accepts inline JSON or a JSON file path, normalizes inline
users to a private temporary file for inherited clients, and removes it after
exit. It keeps the frozen generated handles. Run it on v1, live v2, rollback
and forward; use BACKEND_URL from your own bounded serve proof. Missing day-one
client:calls is explicit: author that business client before claiming coverage.
The frozen script may intentionally fail a newly forbidden call; record the
code separately from transport failures. Do not replace its API with v2's.

```js
import { uniqueName, freshUser } from "snapback4/proof";
const title = uniqueName("proof");
const member = await freshUser(host, {role:"member", provision:api.provision});
// member has email/password/principal/token; freshUser uses real signup and
// the ordinary operator provision mutation. Customize args for your schema.
```

Use freshUser once per role per proof run and reuse its session across that
run's release cycles. uniqueName supplies fresh row names/intent IDs; it cannot
clear a per-owner cap. A cap refusal on a second run needs a fresh owner/group
or deliberate authorized cleanup. Never raise caps or delete the shared
production store just to get green. Capacity refusals remain failures with a
setup hint, never silently retried writes.

## Production roster and complete inspection

`guide api proof` includes startServe, provisionAccounts, account/roster shapes
and exact options. For a persistent production driver use
`guide production --section 'Serve script and reusable accounts'` only. Signup
creates an account; an authorized provisioning mutation grants its role.

```js
import { startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
const host = await startServe("./deploy", {
  provisionUsers:{roles:{member:2, admin:2}, provision:api.provision}
});
const users = host.sessions; // {member:[account,account], admin:[account,account]}
// Private harness readiness: {backendUrl:host.url, users}; do not log elsewhere.
// Stop host in finally, or use your existing serve-check bounded driver.
```

Production provisioning reuses the named roster and logs in on rerun. Keep
./deploy/proof-accounts/default.json (0600) with its store; it holds credentials,
stable provision IDs and partial completion. Use freshUser for isolated test
batches, the persistent roster for production accounts. Neither bypasses rules.

`data TABLE --release ./deploy --all --json` and
`snapshotAll("TABLE", {release:"./deploy"})` include dormant cells and retained
rows, merged once per ID. Active values win; deleted rows stay absent. This is
administrative inspection, bypassing application read rules, not a client
compatibility proof. Ephemeral rows exist only in serving memory. Inspect
`releases ./deploy --json` separately for retained source history.
//! A day-one headless client must keep its own handles and module boundary.
// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#9 — capture before editing; never overwrite historical evidence.
use std::{path::Path, io};
use serde_json::json;

pub(super) fn files(dir: &Path, sources: &[(String, String)]) -> io::Result<Vec<(&'static str, String)>> {
    if dir.join("v1").exists() { return Ok(Vec::new()); }
    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(sources);
    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() { return Err(io::Error::other("cannot freeze an unchecked v1 client; fix current schema first")); }
    let mut files = Vec::new();
    fn push(files: &mut Vec<(&'static str,String)>, path: String, text: String) {
        files.push((Box::leak(path.into_boxed_str()),text));
    }
    fn walk(root: &Path, relative: &Path, files: &mut Vec<(&'static str,String)>) -> io::Result<()> {
        let path = root.join(relative);
        if !path.exists() { return Ok(()); }
        if path.symlink_metadata()?.file_type().is_symlink() { return Err(io::Error::other(format!("frozen client refuses symlink {}", path.display()))); }
        if path.is_dir() {
            for entry in std::fs::read_dir(path)? { walk(root, &relative.join(entry?.file_name()),files)?; }
        } else if matches!(path.extension().and_then(|s|s.to_str()), Some("mjs"|"cjs"|"js"|"ts"|"tsx"|"jsx")) {
            push(files,format!("v1/{}",relative.display()),std::fs::read_to_string(path)?);
        }
        Ok(())
    }
    // Save application modules, not node_modules, stores, credentials or build output.
    for path in ["scripts", "src", "app"] { walk(dir,Path::new(path),&mut files)?; }
    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(dir)? {
        let entry=entry?;
        if entry.path().is_file() && entry.path().extension().is_some_and(|e|matches!(e.to_str(),Some("mjs"|"js"|"ts"|"tsx"))) { walk(dir,Path::new(&entry.file_name()),&mut files)?; }
    }
    for (name,text) in sources { push(&mut files,format!("v1/snapback/{name}"),text.clone()); }
    push(&mut files,"v1/snapback/generated/api.ts".into(),snapback4_lang::emit::typescript(&bound));
    push(&mut files,"v1/snapback/generated/package.json".into(),"{\"type\":\"module\"}\n".into());
    let package: serde_json::Value = std::fs::read(dir.join("package.json")).ok().and_then(|b|serde_json::from_slice(&b).ok()).unwrap_or_default();
    let command=package["scripts"]["client:calls"].as_str();
    let mut frozen=json!({"private":true,"type":"module","scripts":{"client:calls":"node .snapback4-frozen-run.mjs"}});
    if package["scripts"].is_object() { frozen["scripts"]=package["scripts"].clone(); }
    frozen["scripts"]["client:calls"]=json!("node .snapback4-frozen-run.mjs");
    if let Some(command)=command { frozen["scripts"]["client:calls:frozen"]=json!(command); }
    for group in ["dependencies","devDependencies"] { if package[group].is_object() { frozen[group]=package[group].clone(); } }
    push(&mut files,"v1/package.json".into(),super::pretty(&frozen));
    push(&mut files,"v1/.snapback4-frozen-run.mjs".into(),include_str!("frozen-run.mjs").into());
    Ok(files)
}
// API signatures: snapback4 guide api proof | client | auth | generated
// Frozen source uses its saved API. Inline users are adapted for legacy file readers.
import { mkdtemp, writeFile, rm, readFile } from "node:fs/promises";
import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { join, dirname } from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
const cwd = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
const pkg = JSON.parse(await readFile(join(cwd, "package.json"), "utf8"));
if (!pkg.scripts?.["client:calls:frozen"]) throw Error("No day-one client:calls was present at capture; add a business client before claiming frozen-client coverage.");
let temporary;
const env = { ...process.env };
try {
  if (env.CALLS_USERS?.trimStart().startsWith("{")) {
    const users = JSON.parse(env.CALLS_USERS);
    if (!users || Array.isArray(users) || Object.values(users).some(batch => !Array.isArray(batch))) throw Error("CALLS_USERS must be a role-to-account-arrays object");
    temporary = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), "snapback4-v1-users-"));
    env.CALLS_USERS = join(temporary, "users.json");
    await writeFile(env.CALLS_USERS, JSON.stringify(users), {mode:0o600});
  }
  const child = spawn("npm", ["run", "--silent", "client:calls:frozen"], {cwd,env,stdio:"inherit"});
  const signals = ["SIGTERM", "SIGINT"];
  const forward = signal => { try { child.kill(signal); } catch {} };
  const handlers = signals.map(signal => {const fn=()=>forward(signal);process.on(signal,fn);return fn;});
  try {
    const result = await new Promise((resolve,reject)=>{child.once("error",reject);child.once("exit",(code,signal)=>resolve({code,signal}));});
    process.exitCode = result.code ?? 1;
  } finally { signals.forEach((signal,i)=>process.off(signal,handlers[i])); }
} finally { if (temporary) await rm(temporary,{recursive:true,force:true}); }

//! Complete operator inspection over the existing read-only retained views.
// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#9 — retained cells supplement active images once per id.
use super::Options;
use snapback4_core::{inspection::{InspectionCatalog,check_output_names},schema::Schema,store_port::AnyInspection};
use serde_json::{Value,json};
use std::{collections::BTreeMap,path::Path};

type Result<T> = std::result::Result<T,(&'static str,String)>;
pub(super) fn run(path: &Path, table: &str, options: &Options) -> i32 {
    match inspect(path,table) {
        Ok(rows) => {
            if options.json { println!("{}",json!({"rows":rows,"more":false,"limit":null,"inspection":"active and retained records, merged by id"})); }
            else { for row in rows {println!("{row}");} }
            0
        }
        Err((code,message)) => { eprintln!("{code}: {message}"); 1 }
    }
}
fn inspect(path: &Path, table: &str) -> Result<Vec<Value>> {
    // Restrict identifiers before constructing the inspection SELECT.
    if !table.bytes().enumerate().all(|(i,b)|b.is_ascii_alphabetic() || i>0 && (b.is_ascii_digit() || b==b'_')) || table.is_empty() {
        return Err(("E_INPUT","data --all takes one application table name".into()));
    }
    let conn=AnyInspection::open(path)?;
    conn.begin_snapshot()?;
    let (_,encoded)=conn.active_schema_record()?;
    let active:Schema=serde_json::from_str(&encoded).map_err(|e|("E_SCHEMA",e.to_string()))?;
    active.refuse_private(table).map_err(|e|("E_SCHEMA",e.to_string()))?;
    let schema=conn.retained_schema(active.clone())?;
    schema.refuse_private(table).map_err(|e|("E_SCHEMA",e.to_string()))?;
    let definition=schema.table(table).ok_or_else(||("E_SCHEMA",format!("no table {table}")))?;
    if definition.ephemeral.is_some() {return Err(("E_SCHEMA",format!("data {table}: ephemeral rows live only in serving memory; no disk snapshot")));}
    conn.build_retained_views(&schema,false,true,|_|{})?;
    let sql=format!("SELECT * FROM \"{table}\" ORDER BY id, CASE _snapback_state WHEN 'active' THEN 0 WHEN 'dormant-row' THEN 1 ELSE 2 END");
    let records=conn.query(&sql,u32::MAX,true,
        |message|snapback4_core::inspection::query_error(&schema,message),
        |columns|check_output_names(columns,true),
        |columns|super::sql::object_kinds(&sql,&schema,columns,true).map_err(|e|("E_INPUT",e)))?;
    if records.more { return Err(("E_INPUT","data --all inspection exceeded its addressable row count; no complete snapshot".into())); }
    let mut merged:BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Map<String,Value>>=BTreeMap::new();
    for values in records.rows {
        let row:serde_json::Map<String,Value>=records.columns.iter().cloned().zip(values).collect();
        let id=row.get("id").and_then(Value::as_str).ok_or(("E_SCHEMA","retained record missing id".into()))?;
        let provided:Vec<String>=serde_json::from_str(row.get("_snapback_columns").and_then(Value::as_str).unwrap_or("[]")).map_err(|e|("E_SCHEMA",e.to_string()))?;
        let is_active=row.get("_snapback_state").and_then(Value::as_str)==Some("active");
        let target=merged.entry(id.to_owned()).or_default();
        for (key,value) in row.iter().filter(|(key,_)| !key.starts_with("_snapback_")) {
            let live_field=is_active && active.table(table).is_some_and(|t|t.columns.contains_key(key));
            if key=="id" || live_field || provided.contains(key) { target.entry(key.clone()).or_insert_with(||value.clone()); }
        }
    }
    Ok(merged.into_values().map(Value::Object).collect())
}
// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#9 — reruns isolate identities as well as row names.
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
/** A unique row name or intent ID for this run; does not bypass per-owner caps. */
export function uniqueName(prefix = "proof"): string {
  if (!/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_-]{0,31}$/.test(prefix)) throw Error("E_PROOF_INPUT: uniqueName prefix must be 1..32 ASCII name characters, starting with a letter");
  return `${prefix}-${randomUUID()}`;
}
/** Setup advice only; never retries a refused mutation or changes its code. */
export function capacityAdvice(text: string): string {
  return /holds at most \d+ rows/.test(text)
    ? "Proof capacity reached: a repeat run shares rows with earlier runs. Use freshUser() for a fresh owner/group and uniqueName() for row names, or deliberate authorized cleanup. Do not reset the production store or raise its cap for the proof."
    : "";
}
import test from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import {mkdtempSync,mkdirSync,writeFileSync,readFileSync,rmSync} from 'node:fs';
import {tmpdir} from 'node:os';
import {join,resolve} from 'node:path';

import {createClient} from '../dist/client.js';
const root=resolve(import.meta.dirname,'../../..'),env={SNAPBACK4_BIN:join(root,'target/debug/snapback4')};
function project(t,source){const cwd=mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(),'dx16-'));t.after(()=>rmSync(cwd,{recursive:true,force:true}));mkdirSync(join(cwd,'snapback'));writeFileSync(join(cwd,'snapback/schema.q'),source);return cwd;}
const schema=`use identity password
table roles:
  principal: principal
  role: enum('member')
  unique principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:operator'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  online only
mutation provision(principal: principal, role: enum('member')):
  return insert roles {principal,role}
table rows:
  owner: principal
  title: text <=80
  cap 1 per owner
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer and exists roles[principal = viewer]
  online only
mutation add(title: text <=80):
  return insert rows {owner:viewer,title}
query list():
  return rows where .owner = viewer first 20 by .id
`;
test('wave33 Muse 011/005/004: two proof runs use fresh users and names without cap collisions',async t=>{
 const cwd=project(t,schema);await deploy('production','snapback',{cwd,env});const host=await startServe('production',{cwd,env});
 try{const principals=new Set(),names=new Set();for(let run=0;run<2;run++){
  const account=await freshUser(host,{role:'member',provision:{name:'provision'}});assert.ok(!principals.has(account.principal));principals.add(account.principal);
  const title=uniqueName('proof');assert.ok(!names.has(title));names.add(title);const client=createClient({url:host.url,session:account});
  try{const write=await client.mutate({name:'add',kind:'mutation'},{title});assert.equal(write.state,'sent',JSON.stringify(write));}finally{client.close();}
 }
 const roleless=await freshUser(host);assert.equal(roleless.role,'users');assert.ok(roleless.token);
 }finally{await host.stop();}
 assert.equal((await snapshotAll('rows',{cwd,env,release:'production'})).length,2);
 assert.throws(()=>uniqueName('../bad'),/E_PROOF_INPUT/);
});

//! Disposable reading history contains hashes, never schema or account contents.
// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#8-wave-32-builder-reading-and-waiting-tok1 — repeated reads emit only new complete blocks.
use std::{collections::BTreeSet, path::Path};
use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};

pub fn render(name: &str, text: &str, force: bool) -> String {
    let Ok(cwd) = std::env::current_dir() else { return text.into(); };
    let Some(root) = cwd.ancestors().find(|p| p.join("snapback").is_dir() || std::fs::read(p.join("package.json")).ok().and_then(|bytes| serde_json::from_slice::<serde_json::Value>(&bytes).ok()).is_some_and(|package| ["dependencies", "devDependencies"].iter().any(|key| package[*key]["snapback4"].is_string()))) else { return text.into(); };
    render_in(root, name, text, force)
}
pub(crate) fn render_in(root: &Path, name: &str, text: &str, force: bool) -> String {
    let path = root.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json");
    let mut seen: BTreeSet<String> = std::fs::read(&path).ok().and_then(|b| serde_json::from_slice(&b).ok()).unwrap_or_default();
    let blocks = snapback4_lang::guide::reading_blocks(text);
    let mut new = Vec::new(); let mut already = 0;
    for block in &blocks {
        let hash = format!("{:x}", Sha256::digest(format!("{name}\0{block}").as_bytes()));
        if seen.insert(hash) { new.push(block.as_str()); } else { already += 1; }
    }
    // A lost concurrent history update merely reprints text; it cannot hide new content.
    let write = || -> std::io::Result<()> {
        std::fs::create_dir_all(path.parent().unwrap())?;
        let tmp = path.with_extension(format!("{}.tmp", std::process::id()));
        std::fs::write(&tmp, serde_json::to_vec(&seen)?)?;
        std::fs::rename(&tmp, &path)
    };
    if write().is_err() || force || already == 0 { return text.into(); }
    format!("already shown: {name}, {already} blocks; new: {} blocks (snapback4 guide {name} --repeat forces this read; --full the reference)\n\n{}", new.len(), new.join("\n"))
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    #[test]
    fn wave32_muse_019_028_repeated_sections_keep_changed_recipe_and_force() {
        let root = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("tok1-history-{}", std::process::id()));
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
        let first = "# Uploads\n\nOwn bytes.\n\n```js\nawait upload(bytes);\n\nawait place();\n```\n";
        assert_eq!(render_in(&root,"uploads",first,false), first);
        assert!(render_in(&root,"uploads",first,false).contains("new: 0 blocks"));
        assert_eq!(render_in(&root,"another-card",first,false), first, "a first card read keeps shared context");
        let changed = first.replace("place()", "placeCopy()");
        let delta = render_in(&root,"uploads",&changed,false);
        assert!(delta.contains("```js\nawait upload(bytes);\n\nawait placeCopy();\n```"));
        assert_eq!(render_in(&root,"uploads",&changed,true), changed);
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(root).unwrap();
    }
}
//! Declaration digests are generated by the client build; no manually copied signatures.
// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#8-wave-32-builder-reading-and-waiting-tok1 — on-demand module shapes replace d.ts greps.
pub fn render(module: &str, full: bool) -> Result<String, String> {
    match module {
        "proof" if full => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/proof_api_reference.md").into()),
        "proof" => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_proof.md").into()),
        "client" if full => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_client_full.md").into()),
        "client" => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_client.md").into()),
        "auth" if full => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_auth_full.md").into()),
        "auth" => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_auth.md").into()),
        "generated" => {
            let cwd = std::env::current_dir().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let root = cwd.ancestors().find(|p| p.join("snapback").is_dir()).ok_or("guide api generated: run in your project after snapback4 check")?;
            let api = std::fs::read_to_string(root.join("snapback/generated/api.ts")).map_err(|_| "guide api generated: run snapback4 check first")?;
            let index = api.lines().skip(1).take_while(|l| !l.starts_with("// Full signatures")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n");
            if !index.contains("Operation index") { return Err("guide api generated: regenerate api.ts with snapback4 check".into()); }
            Ok(format!("# Your checked API\n\n{index}\n\nImport api from ./snapback/generated/api.ts. Use api.<operation> with its named argument object. Full ArgsOf/ResultOf and row types live below that file's index.\n"))
        }
        _ => Err("usage: snapback4 guide api <proof|client|auth|generated>".into()),
    }
}
import { capacityAdvice } from "./proof-fresh.ts";
import { rosterLock } from "./proof-roster-lock.ts";
import { identityRequest } from "./identity-request.ts";
import { randomUUID, randomBytes } from "node:crypto";
import { mkdirSync, openSync, closeSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync, renameSync, unlinkSync, lstatSync, fsyncSync } from "node:fs";
import { join, resolve } from "node:path";
import type { ServeHost } from "./proof.ts";

export interface StagingAccount {
  kind: "user"; role: string; email: string; password: string; principal: string; token: string; expiresAt: number;
}
export type StagingSessions = Record<string, StagingAccount[]>;
export type ProvisionedAccount = StagingAccount;
export type ProvisionedSessions = StagingSessions;
export interface ProvisionAccountsOptions extends SeedAccountsOptions {
  /** Named persistent roster within this deployment. Default default. */
  roster?: string;
  /** Required for a host not returned by startServe. Keep with the deployment. */
  rosterDir?: string;
}
export interface SeedAccountsOptions {
  roles?: Record<string, number>;
  provision?: { readonly name: string };
  /** Additional password users with no provision call; returned under sessions.users. */
  users?: number;
  /** Service seat authorized by the provisioning mutation. Default operator. */
  service?: string;
  args?: (account: StagingAccount) => Record<string, unknown>;
  /** Whole setup deadline, including rate-limit waits. Default 5 minutes. */
  timeoutMs?: number;
}

function validatePlan(options: SeedAccountsOptions) {
  // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#8-wave-18-developer-loop-amendment-dx4 — custom argument keys override defaults; the seat grants no extra authority.
  const service = options.service ?? "operator";
  const roles = Object.entries(options.roles ?? {}), users=options.users ?? 0, timeout = options.timeoutMs ?? 300_000;
  if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$/.test(service) || !Number.isSafeInteger(timeout) || timeout < 1 || !Number.isSafeInteger(users) || users<0 || (!roles.length&&!users) || (users>0&&roles.some(([role])=>role==="users")) || roles.some(([role, n]) => !/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_-]{0,63}$/.test(role) || !Number.isSafeInteger(n) || n < 1)
      || roles.reduce((sum, [, n]) => sum + n, users) > 100 || (roles.length>0 || options.provision!==undefined) && (typeof options.provision?.name!=="string" || !/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*(?:\.[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*)*$/.test(options.provision.name))) {
    throw Error("E_STAGING_INPUT: provide 1..100 accounts across named roles, a mutation handle and a positive timeoutMs");
  }
  return {service, roles:[...roles,...(users ? [["users",users] as [string,number]] : [])], timeout};
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#2-real-staging-accounts — real credentials and ordinary operator mutations, never system seeding or auth bypass.
/** @throws E_STAGING_INPUT for invalid plans; E_STAGING for production/dev hosts; E_STAGING_TIMEOUT, E_STAGING_SIGNUP or E_STAGING_PROVISION for incomplete setup. Partial accounts/grants may exist; inspect or discard the staging store before retrying. */
async function accounts(host: Pick<ServeHost, "url" | "operatorToken">, options: SeedAccountsOptions, environment: "staging" | "production" | "development", roster?: Roster, save: () => void = () => {}, assertHeld: () => void = () => {}): Promise<StagingSessions> {
  const {service, roles, timeout} = validatePlan(options);
  const end = performance.now() + timeout;
  const partial = environment === "development" ? "inspect partial development accounts/grants or discard this development store" : environment === "staging" ? "inspect or discard this staging deployment" : "inspect partial production accounts/grants before retrying";
  const remaining = () => {
    assertHeld();
    const ms = Math.ceil(end - performance.now());
    if (ms <= 0) throw Error(`E_STAGING_TIMEOUT: setup incomplete; partial accounts/grants may exist; ${partial}`);
    return ms;
  };
  const guardedFetch: typeof fetch = (url, init) => { assertHeld(); return fetch(url, init); };
  const request = (path: string, init?: RequestInit) => guardedFetch(host.url + path, { ...init, signal: AbortSignal.timeout(Math.min(remaining(), 30_000)) });
  const config = await request("/auth/config");
  const identity = await config.json();
  if (!config.ok || identity.environment !== environment || identity.development !== (environment === "development") || !identity.password) {
    throw Error(environment === "development" ? "E_DEV_ACCOUNTS: devAccounts requires startDev with password identity; serve hosts refuse before creating users" : environment === "staging"
      ? "E_STAGING: account seeding requires serve on a fresh deploy --staging with password identity; production and dev refuse before creating users"
      : "E_PRODUCTION_ACCOUNTS: account provisioning requires serve on a production deploy with password identity; staging and dev refuse before creating users");
  }
  const operator = Object.keys(options.roles??{}).length ? await host.operatorToken(service) : "";
  if (environment === "production" && operator) {
    const response = await request("/schema", { headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${operator}` } }), schema = await response.json();
    const named = response.ok && Array.isArray(schema.service_identities) && schema.service_identities.includes(`service:${service}`);
    if (!named) process.stderr.write(`snapback4: WARNING minted service:${service}, but the served schema reports no explicit positive authorization mention for that service identity; rules still decide every operation and provisionAccounts(${options.provision?.name}) will likely refuse.\n`);
  }
  const sessions: StagingSessions = Object.create(null);
  for (const [role, count] of roles) {
    sessions[role] = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
      let fresh = false;
      let entry = roster?.entries.find(entry => entry.role === role && entry.index === i);
      if (!entry) {
        fresh = true;
        entry = { role, index: i, email: `${environment}-${role}-${randomUUID()}@example.test`, password: randomBytes(24).toString("base64url"), id: randomUUID(), done: false };
        roster?.entries.push(entry); save(); // Persist identity before the first possibly ambiguous write.
      }
      const { email, password } = entry;
      let reply: Record<string, unknown>;
      if (roster && !fresh) {
        const result = await identityRequest(host.url + "/auth/login", {
          method:"POST", headers:{"content-type":"application/json"}, body:JSON.stringify({email,password}),
        }, guardedFetch, {timeoutMs:remaining()}).catch(error => {
          if ((error as {code?:string})?.code === "E_IDENTITY_TIMEOUT") throw Error(`E_STAGING_TIMEOUT: login deadline expired; ${partial}`);
          remaining(); throw error;
        });
        reply = result.json;
        if (!reply.session && (entry.principal || (reply.denied as {code?:string})?.code !== "E_AUTH")) throw Error("E_PRODUCTION_ROSTER: saved account login failed; inspect roster and deployment; no replacement account created");
      } else reply = {};
      if (!reply.session) {
      const result = await identityRequest(host.url + "/auth/signup", {
        method:"POST", headers:{"content-type":"application/json"}, body:JSON.stringify({email,password}),
      }, guardedFetch, {timeoutMs:remaining()}).catch(error => {
        if ((error as {code?: string})?.code === "E_IDENTITY_TIMEOUT") throw Error(`E_STAGING_TIMEOUT: signup deadline expired; partial accounts/grants may exist; ${partial}`);
        remaining(); throw error;
      });
      reply = result.json;
      }
      if ((reply.denied as {code?:string} | undefined)?.code === "E_RATE_LIMIT") {
        throw Error("E_STAGING_TIMEOUT: identity retry budget exhausted; increase timeoutMs or inspect partial accounts/grants before retrying");
      }
      const session = reply.session as {principal?:string;kind?:string;token?:string;expiresAt?:number} | undefined;
      if (typeof session?.principal !== "string" || typeof session.token !== "string" || typeof session.expiresAt !== "number" || session.kind !== "user") {
        throw Error(`E_STAGING_SIGNUP: signup refused (${(reply.denied as {code?:string} | undefined)?.code ?? "invalid reply"}); partial accounts/grants may exist; ${partial}`);
      }
      const account: StagingAccount = { kind: session.kind, role, email, password, principal: session.principal, token: session.token, expiresAt: session.expiresAt };
      if (entry.principal && entry.principal !== account.principal) throw Error("E_PRODUCTION_ROSTER: principal differs from saved roster; inspect deployment");
      entry.principal = account.principal; save();
      if (role==="users" && (options.users??0)>0) {entry.done=true;save();sessions[role]!.push(account);continue;}
      const args = { principal: account.principal, role, ...options.args?.(account) };
      const encodedArgs = canonical(args);
      if (entry.args && entry.args !== encodedArgs) throw Error("E_PRODUCTION_ROSTER: provisioning arguments changed; inspect roster instead of silently reassigning roles");
      entry.args = encodedArgs; save();
      if (!entry.done) {
      const response = await request(`/m/${options.provision?.name}`, { method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${operator}` }, body: JSON.stringify({ id: entry.id, args }) });
      const result = await response.json();
      if (!response.ok || result.state !== "sent") {
        let parameters:string[]=[];
        try {
          const response=await request("/schema",{headers:{authorization:`Bearer ${operator}`}}), schema=await response.json();
          const program=schema.programs?.find((p:{name:string})=>p.name===options.provision?.name);
          parameters=Object.keys(program?.args??{}).sort();
        } catch { /* failure diagnostics must not mask the original refusal */ }
        const merged=Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(args).map(([key,value])=>[key,`<redacted ${value===null?"null":Array.isArray(value)?"array":typeof value}>`]));
        const refusal = result.why ?? result.denied;
        // Preserve the backend's explanation without echoing opaque arguments.
        const strings = [account.email, account.password, account.token], scalars = new Set<string>();
        const opaque = (value: unknown): void => {
          if (typeof value === "string") { if (value) strings.push(value); }
          else if (value === null || typeof value === "number" || typeof value === "boolean") scalars.add(String(value));
          else if (Array.isArray(value)) value.forEach(opaque);
          else if (value && typeof value === "object") Object.values(value).forEach(opaque);
        };
        opaque(args);
        let explanation: string = typeof refusal?.message === "string" ? refusal.message.slice(0, 4096) : "";
        for (const value of strings.sort((a, b) => b.length - a.length)) {
          if (value.length > 3) { explanation = explanation.replaceAll(value, "<redacted>"); continue; }
          // A one-letter selection must not turn "argument" into gibberish.
          // Redact quoted/standalone echoes while retaining named schema sites.
          const escaped = value.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, "\\$&");
          explanation = explanation.replace(new RegExp(`(?<![\\p{L}\\p{N}_.])${escaped}(?![\\p{L}\\p{N}_])`, "gu"), (token, offset: number, text: string) =>
            /(?:argument|field|column|table|operation|mutation|code)\s+["']?$/.test(text.slice(0, offset)) ? token : "<redacted>");
        }
        // Backend value descriptions use scalar tokens ("the number 482719").
        // Quoted scalar echoes are values too. Preserve explicitly named
        // schema sites and whole identifiers/codes, not arbitrary quoted values.
        explanation = explanation.replace(/"(?:\\.|[^"\\])*"|'(?:\\.|[^'\\])*'|[\p{L}_][\p{L}\p{N}_]*|-?(?:\d+(?:\.\d*)?|\.\d+)(?:[eE][+-]?\d+)?/gu,
          (token, offset: number, text: string) => {
            const quoted = token[0] === '"' || token[0] === "'", value = quoted ? token.slice(1, -1) : token;
            const schemaSite = /^[\p{L}_][\p{L}\p{N}_]*$/u.test(value)
              && /(?:argument|field|column|table|operation|mutation|code)\s+$/.test(text.slice(0, offset));
            if (!scalars.has(value) || schemaSite) return token;
            return quoted ? `${token[0]}<redacted>${token[0]}` : "<redacted>";
          });
        throw Error(`E_STAGING_PROVISION: ${options.provision?.name} refused (${refusal?.code ?? "invalid reply"})${explanation ? `: ${explanation}` : ""}; supplied arguments: ${Object.keys(args).sort().join(", ")}; merged args (opaque values redacted): ${JSON.stringify(merged)}; mutation parameters: ${parameters.length?parameters.join(", "):"unavailable"}; args(account) merges over {principal, role}, explicit keys win; ${partial}${capacityAdvice(explanation ?? "") ? "; " + capacityAdvice(explanation ?? "") : ""}`);
      }
      entry.done = true; save();
      }
      sessions[role]!.push(account);
    }
  }
  return sessions;
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#2-real-staging-accounts — real credentials and ordinary operator mutations, never system seeding or auth bypass.
/** @throws E_STAGING_INPUT for invalid plans; E_STAGING for production/dev hosts; E_STAGING_TIMEOUT, E_STAGING_SIGNUP or E_STAGING_PROVISION for incomplete setup. */
export function seedAccounts(host: Pick<ServeHost, "url" | "operatorToken">, options: SeedAccountsOptions): Promise<StagingSessions> {
  return accounts(host, options, "staging");
}

function canonical(value: unknown): string {
  // Compare the JSON wire values, including nested objects, independent of key order.
  const sorted = (v: unknown): unknown => Array.isArray(v) ? v.map(sorted) : v && typeof v === "object"
    ? Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(v).sort(([a],[b]) => a < b ? -1 : a > b ? 1 : 0).map(([k,x]) => [k,sorted(x)])) : v;
  return JSON.stringify(sorted(JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(value))));
}
interface RosterEntry { role: string; index: number; email: string; password: string; id: string; principal?: string; args?: string; done: boolean }
interface Roster { version: 1; plan: string; entries: RosterEntry[] }
/** Reuse a named deployment roster with fresh password sessions; provision only unfinished entries.
 * @throws E_STAGING_INPUT for invalid plans; E_PRODUCTION_ACCOUNTS for staging/dev;
 * E_PRODUCTION_ROSTER for changed/unsafe roster or failed saved login; E_PRODUCTION_ROSTER_BUSY for concurrent setup;
 * E_STAGING_TIMEOUT, E_STAGING_SIGNUP or E_STAGING_PROVISION for incomplete setup.
 */
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#16-wave-27-process-checks-dx10 — persist credentials and stable IDs before production effects.
export async function provisionAccounts(host: Pick<ServeHost, "url" | "operatorToken"> & { deploymentDir?: string }, options: ProvisionAccountsOptions): Promise<ProvisionedSessions> {
  validatePlan(options);
  const name = options.roster ?? "default", directory = options.rosterDir ?? host.deploymentDir;
  if (!directory || !/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$/.test(name)) throw Error("E_PRODUCTION_ROSTER: use startServe or supply rosterDir, and a simple roster name");
  const base = resolve(directory);
  if (!lstatSync(base).isDirectory()) throw Error("E_PRODUCTION_ROSTER: rosterDir must be an existing deployment directory");
  const folder = join(base, "proof-accounts"); mkdirSync(folder, {mode:0o700, recursive:true});
  // Persist the newly created roster directory's link as well as each snapshot.
  const parent = openSync(base, "r"); try { fsyncSync(parent); } finally { closeSync(parent); }
  const path = join(folder, name + ".json"), lock = path + ".lock";
  const owned = rosterLock(lock);
  try {
    const plan = JSON.stringify({...(options.users?{users:options.users}:{}),roles:Object.entries(options.roles??{}).sort(), provision:options.provision?.name, service:options.service ?? "operator"});
    let roster: Roster;
    try {
      if (!lstatSync(path).isFile() || (lstatSync(path).mode & 0o077)) throw Error("unsafe roster file");
      roster = JSON.parse(readFileSync(path, "utf8"));
      if (roster.version !== 1 || typeof roster.plan !== "string" || !Array.isArray(roster.entries)) throw Error("invalid roster");
      if (roster.plan !== plan) {
        // Render only validated plan fields, never arbitrary saved strings or entries.
        const pinned = JSON.parse(roster.plan);
        if (pinned.provision !== undefined && typeof pinned.provision !== "string") throw Error("invalid roster plan");
        const normalized = validatePlan({...pinned, roles: Object.fromEntries(pinned.roles), provision: pinned.provision !== undefined ? {name:pinned.provision} : undefined});
        const safe = JSON.stringify({...(pinned.users?{users:pinned.users}:{}),roles:Object.entries(Object.fromEntries(pinned.roles)).sort(),provision:pinned.provision,service:normalized.service});
        throw Object.assign(Error(`E_PRODUCTION_ROSTER: supplied plan ${plan}; pinned plan ${safe}; reuse the pinned plan or intentionally choose a new roster name; no new accounts created`), {planMismatch:true});
      }
      const slots = new Set<string>();
      for (const entry of roster.entries) {
        if (!entry) throw Error("invalid roster entry");
        const slot = `${entry.role}:${entry.index}`;
        if (!entry || typeof entry.role !== "string" || !Number.isSafeInteger(entry.index) || entry.index < 0
            || entry.index >= (entry.role==="users"&&options.users ? options.users : options.roles?.[entry.role] ?? 0) || slots.has(slot)
            || typeof entry.email !== "string" || !entry.email.endsWith("@example.test") || typeof entry.password !== "string" || entry.password.length < 20
            || typeof entry.id !== "string" || !/^[a-f0-9-]{36}$/.test(entry.id) || typeof entry.done !== "boolean"
            || (entry.principal !== undefined && typeof entry.principal !== "string") || (entry.args !== undefined && typeof entry.args !== "string")
            || (entry.done && (!entry.principal || !(entry.role==="users"&&(options.users??0)>0)&&!entry.args))) throw Error("invalid roster entry");
        slots.add(slot);
      }
    } catch (error) {
      if ((error as {planMismatch?:boolean}).planMismatch) throw error;
      if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "ENOENT") throw Error("E_PRODUCTION_ROSTER: unreadable, unsafe or changed roster; inspect it; no new accounts created");
      roster = {version:1, plan, entries:[]};
    }
    const save = () => {
      owned.assertHeld();
      const temporary = path + "." + randomUUID() + ".tmp";
      const snapshot = openSync(temporary, "wx", 0o600);
      try { writeFileSync(snapshot, JSON.stringify(roster)); fsyncSync(snapshot); } finally { closeSync(snapshot); }
      renameSync(temporary, path);
      const parent = openSync(folder, "r"); try { fsyncSync(parent); } finally { closeSync(parent); }
    };
    return await accounts(host, options, "production", roster, save, () => owned.assertHeld());
  } finally { owned.release(); }
}

//! Disposable reading history contains hashes, never schema or account contents.
// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#8-wave-32-builder-reading-and-waiting-tok1 — repeated reads emit only new complete blocks.
use std::{collections::BTreeSet, path::Path};
use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};

pub fn render(name: &str, text: &str, force: bool) -> String {
    let Ok(cwd) = std::env::current_dir() else { return text.into(); };
    let Some(root) = cwd.ancestors().find(|p| p.join("snapback").is_dir() || std::fs::read(p.join("package.json")).ok().and_then(|bytes| serde_json::from_slice::<serde_json::Value>(&bytes).ok()).is_some_and(|package| ["dependencies", "devDependencies"].iter().any(|key| package[*key]["snapback4"].is_string()))) else { return text.into(); };
    render_in(root, name, text, force)
}
pub(crate) fn render_in(root: &Path, name: &str, text: &str, force: bool) -> String {
    let path = root.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json");
    let mut seen: BTreeSet<String> = std::fs::read(&path).ok().and_then(|b| serde_json::from_slice(&b).ok()).unwrap_or_default();
    let blocks = snapback4_lang::guide::reading_blocks(text);
    let mut new = Vec::new(); let mut already = 0;
    for block in &blocks {
        let hash = format!("{:x}", Sha256::digest(format!("{name}\0{block}").as_bytes()));
        if seen.insert(hash) { new.push(block.as_str()); } else { already += 1; }
    }
    // A lost concurrent history update merely reprints text; it cannot hide new content.
    let write = || -> std::io::Result<()> {
        std::fs::create_dir_all(path.parent().unwrap())?;
        let tmp = path.with_extension(format!("{}.tmp", std::process::id()));
        std::fs::write(&tmp, serde_json::to_vec(&seen)?)?;
        std::fs::rename(&tmp, &path)
    };
    if write().is_err() || force || already == 0 { return text.into(); }
    format!("already shown: {name}, {already} blocks; new: {} blocks (snapback4 guide {name} --repeat forces this read; --full the reference)\n\n{}", new.len(), new.join("\n"))
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    #[test]
    fn wave32_muse_019_028_repeated_sections_keep_changed_recipe_and_force() {
        let root = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("tok1-history-{}", std::process::id()));
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
        let first = "# Uploads\n\nOwn bytes.\n\n```js\nawait upload(bytes);\n\nawait place();\n```\n";
        assert_eq!(render_in(&root,"uploads",first,false), first);
        assert!(render_in(&root,"uploads",first,false).contains("new: 0 blocks"));
        assert_eq!(render_in(&root,"another-card",first,false), first, "a first card read keeps shared context");
        let changed = first.replace("place()", "placeCopy()");
        let delta = render_in(&root,"uploads",&changed,false);
        assert!(delta.contains("```js\nawait upload(bytes);\n\nawait placeCopy();\n```"));
        assert_eq!(render_in(&root,"uploads",&changed,true), changed);
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(root).unwrap();
    }
}
//! Declaration digests are generated by the client build; no manually copied signatures.
// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#8-wave-32-builder-reading-and-waiting-tok1 — on-demand module shapes replace d.ts greps.
pub fn render(module: &str, full: bool) -> Result<String, String> {
    match module {
        "proof" if full => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/proof_api_reference.md").into()),
        "proof" => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_proof.md").into()),
        "client" if full => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_client_full.md").into()),
        "client" => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_client.md").into()),
        "auth" if full => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_auth_full.md").into()),
        "auth" => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_auth.md").into()),
        "generated" => {
            let cwd = std::env::current_dir().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let root = cwd.ancestors().find(|p| p.join("snapback").is_dir()).ok_or("guide api generated: run in your project after snapback4 check")?;
            let api = std::fs::read_to_string(root.join("snapback/generated/api.ts")).map_err(|_| "guide api generated: run snapback4 check first")?;
            let index = api.lines().skip(1).take_while(|l| !l.starts_with("// Full signatures")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n");
            if !index.contains("Operation index") { return Err("guide api generated: regenerate api.ts with snapback4 check".into()); }
            Ok(format!("# Your checked API\n\n{index}\n\nImport api from ./snapback/generated/api.ts. Use api.<operation> with its named argument object. Full ArgsOf/ResultOf and row types live below that file's index.\n"))
        }
        _ => Err("usage: snapback4 guide api <proof|client|auth|generated>".into()),
    }
}

/// `--section`/`--find` with an alias of this card that no heading or line
/// holds: the section it names, or the card's opening for a whole-card alias.
fn alias_inside(name: &str, card: &str, words: &str) -> Option<String> {
    if let Some(landing) = alias_landing(words, name, card) { return Some(landing); }
    if super::resolve(words.trim()) != Some(name) || KEYWORDS.contains(&words.trim()) { return None; }
    let found = sections(card);
    let lines: Vec<&str> = card.lines().collect();
    Some(format!("{} is an alias of the whole guide {name} card, which no line names; here is its opening (the rest: --sections, --full):\n\n{}",
        words.trim(), lines[..opening_end(card, &found)].join("\n").trim_end()))
}

fn distance(a: &str, b: &str) -> usize {
    let b: Vec<char> = b.chars().collect();
    let mut row: Vec<usize> = (0..=b.len()).collect();
    for (i, a) in a.chars().enumerate() {
        let mut diagonal = row[0];
        row[0] = i + 1;
        for (j, b) in b.iter().enumerate() {
            let old = row[j + 1];
            row[j + 1] = (diagonal + usize::from(a != *b)).min(row[j] + 1).min(old + 1);
            diagonal = old;
        }
    }
    row[b.len()]
}

/// Rank names and aliases first, then exact words in card contents, then typos.
pub fn nearest(word: &str) -> Vec<&'static str> {
    let word = word.to_lowercase();
    let score = |term: &str| (!term.contains(&word) && !word.contains(term), distance(&word, term));
    let mut cards: Vec<_> = KEYWORDS.iter().map(|&card| {
        let best = ALIASES.iter().filter(|(_, target)| *target == card).map(|(alias, _)| score(alias)).chain([score(card)]).min().unwrap();
        let in_card = super::card(card).is_some_and(|text| text.split(|c: char| !c.is_alphanumeric()).any(|token| token.eq_ignore_ascii_case(&word)));
        let rank = if !best.0 { 0 } else if in_card { 1 } else { 2 };
        ((rank, in_card && card != "language", best.1), card)
    }).collect();
    cards.sort_unstable();
    let mut nearest: Vec<_> = cards.into_iter().take(3).map(|(_, card)| card).collect();
    if super::LANGUAGE.split(|c: char| !c.is_alphanumeric()).any(|token| token.eq_ignore_ascii_case(&word)) && !nearest.contains(&"language") {
        nearest[2] = "language";
    }
    nearest
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#21 — extraction widening and guarded rollback use the reviewed release contract.
pub(super) const EVOLUTION: &str = r##"# Evolution: adopting a schema

Schema adoption applies a new backend definition to existing data.
The stored rows must fit the new types, keys and constraints.

```quarry
table notes:
  body: text <=200
  archived: bool default false  -- fills this field in older rows
  label: text <=40 ?            -- older rows read null
  read <- .owner = viewer
  owner: principal
```

New optional columns, required columns with literal defaults, non-unique
indexes, tables and operations adopt without ceremony. Literal defaults
fill older row images on the server and device, including in new indexes,
without rewriting stored rows or logging partition events.

Required columns without literal defaults, tighter types, unique indexes
and stronger constraints need the existing rows to fit. A new required
`default now` or `default viewer` column needs an empty table.

## Operations

Run `snapback4 check` against the dev store, or `check --store <path>`:
each failed successor step is E_ADOPT at its column, index or table, with
a rewrite. `dev` refuses that step on save and keeps the previous generation.
A green check means the current rows fit; a later write can change that.
Without a store, check only compiles.

## Release rollback and hot activation

`deploy <dir>` and `rollback <dir> [--to N]` activate atomically while `serve`
is running. Rollback keeps removed durable tables, columns and index definitions
as dormant storage, invisible to old programs and sync. Forward restores their
values. Older updates preserve dormant cells; deletion removes them. New older-
release rows receive the forward literal default or optional null. A required
field can declare a conditional/derived `backfill` in the candidate schema;
deploy previews exact transformed-row counts and commits the patch with the
release. Keep declarations across later releases; rollback restores untouched
preimages or names a conflict, and forward catches up older writes. Contextual
defaults never invent history. `guide deploy` contains the complete persistent
production recipe. REL5 owns release selection and compatibility narration.

An incompatible enum/text/type value becomes a dormant row during release activation,
with its original values restored on compatible forward. Dev adoption still refuses
incompatible values with E_ADOPT. Restored uniqueness
and parent-lifetime/audience checks can also refuse; failure keeps the old release.
`--allow-destructive` never bypasses these checks.

Reusing dormant names with new source requires `deploy <dir> --restore-dormant`;
`rollback --to N` explicitly selects an immutable release and restoration.
`prune <dir>` previews counts/bytes/ids; `prune <dir> --allow-destructive`
permanently removes all dormant storage. Assets stay retained but unreadable
while dormant. `releases <dir>` inspects database-authoritative history.

Clients reacquire a fresh generation for changed execution content, including
native-only module changes. A failed hot adoption leaves the old release serving.
For a truthful scalar old view, declare `down table name(typedInputs): {oldField: expression}` in newer source. Saved old preimages win; receipts name their rows. Computed down cannot manufacture files/images, collections, references or missing tables. For required → optional media/money and empty albums, `rollback DIR --to N --check --bridge NEW_DIR` generates a current-workflow repair source and checklist with loosened domains and retained new tables. Review nullable UI reads, current safety guards and the checklist, then preview/deploy that NEW release with `deploy DIR --schema NEW_DIR --restore-dormant --check`. New tables may still have no old read path. `table newName renamed from oldName:` preserves table/row/reference identity across both directions; asset-column renames preserve the single asset home. Cross-table indexed existence/unique lookups and migration-only `fileText` are declarative inputs; see the packed-tested language example and `guide deploy`.

Full lifecycle, concurrency, failure and recovery semantics:
[SERVER.md — Release lifecycle](SERVER.md#release-lifecycle).

### Move media into a child table

Save the previous source before removing `orders.scan`. The new child retains
private reads through the same parent. `customer renamed from buyer` carries
reference lifetime evidence; it does not convert the reference type.

```quarry
table people:
  read <- deny
  online only
table orders:
  customer: people renamed from buyer
  owner: principal
  immutable owner
  read <- .owner = viewer
  online only
table scans:
  order: orders on delete restrict
  scan: file <=100000
  immutable order
  read through .order
  online only
extract scans from orders via order: {scan:scan}
```

Preview the whole migration with deploy --check. Initial child ids equal parent
ids; independent destinations refuse. Bytes move without reuploading. Exact
scalar reversal needs a valid parent lifetime and one unambiguous child. A
compatible registered current-workflow view can retain v2-native parents with
several children on default rollback; otherwise `rollback --bridge` supplies a
repair source and checklist. Preview before selecting it.

An ordinary extracted payload may widen required → optional while retaining its
underlying kind, bounds and lifetime; reversal still validates the old required
payload. Keep its read policy: a compatible stricter child policy needs no
intermediate bridge release. Missing bytes, other incompatible type changes or
broader read audiences refuse atomically. Image and bounded asset-list columns
use the same ordinary extraction form.

### One record per photo in a list

For a historical `albums.photos: [image <=10000] <=8`, declare the complete
new source below. The `each` modifier moves one element into each child;
plain `extract` moves a whole list into one child. Element types must match.

```quarry
use identity password
table albums:
  owner: principal
  immutable owner
  read <- .owner = viewer
  delete <- .owner = viewer
  online only
table albumPhotos:
  album: albums on delete cascade
  photo: image <=10000
  immutable album
  cap 8 by album
  read through .album
  online only
extract albumPhotos from albums via album each: {photo:photos}
mutation removeAlbum(album: albums):
  delete albums[album]
```

Child IDs stay stable across rollback/forward and old-client reordering.
Reverse selection collects current child photos, including native v2 children,
in receipt order with native children appended by ID. It moves their homes
back into the old list; it never truncates an old list to its declared bound.
An unrepresentable population becomes dormant under the ordinary old-domain
rules. Empty lists create no children. Repeated asset IDs refuse because one
asset cannot have two homes. Missing bytes, independent destination history,
pruned children, invalid parent lifetimes and broader audiences refuse atomically.
If populated children are dormant under a narrowed child domain, reverse
selection refuses rather than omitting their photos. Restore those rows with
a loosened child-domain bridge first; migration-owned null removals stay dormant.
A temporarily retired child table resumes its validated current homes under the
same extraction; it never replays consumed lists or recaptures independent children.

This is `guide evolution`; client development commands are in CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §5."##;

/// A card's `##`/`###` headings outside code fences: (level, heading, first line, end line).
pub fn sections(card: &str) -> Vec<(usize, String, usize, usize)> {
    let lines: Vec<&str> = card.lines().collect();
    let mut fenced = false;
    let mut found: Vec<(usize, String, usize, usize)> = Vec::new();
    for (index, line) in lines.iter().enumerate() {
        if line.trim_start().starts_with("```") { fenced = !fenced; continue; }
        if fenced { continue; }
        let level = line.chars().take_while(|c| *c == '#').count();
        if !(2..=3).contains(&level) || !line[level..].starts_with(' ') { continue; }
        found.push((level, line[level..].trim().to_owned(), index, lines.len()));
    }
    // A section runs to the next heading at its level or above.
    for i in 0..found.len() {
        let level = found[i].0;
        if let Some(next) = found[i + 1..].iter().find(|next| next.0 <= level) { found[i].3 = next.2; }
    }
    found
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — a card longer than a tool window prints its opening and a numbered index; --full prints it whole.
/// Cards up to this many lines print whole. Above it, `guide <card>` prints
/// the card's opening and a numbered section index: agents batch several
/// cards per command and their tools truncate long output (the start card,
/// 124 lines at most, stays whole).
pub const FULL_LINES: usize = 240;
/// Dense cards with very long lines need the same bounded first read.
pub const FULL_BYTES: usize = 8_000;
/// The most opening lines the overview prints before its section index.
const OPENING_LINES: usize = 40;

/// Lowercase words, punctuation and backticks dropped: `sync to` == "Sync to".
fn words_of(text: &str) -> String {
    text.chars().map(|c| if c.is_alphanumeric() { c.to_ascii_lowercase() } else { ' ' }).collect::<String>().split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ")
}

/// Where the opening (the text before the first `##`) ends.
fn opening_end(card: &str, found: &[(usize, String, usize, usize)]) -> usize {
    found.first().map(|s| s.2).unwrap_or_else(|| card.lines().count())
}

fn index_lines(card: &str, found: &[(usize, String, usize, usize)]) -> String {
    let opening = opening_end(card, found);
    let mut out = String::new();
    if opening > 5 { out.push_str(&format!("    0. Opening ({opening} lines)\n")); }
    for (n, (level, heading, start, end)) in found.iter().enumerate() {
        out.push_str(&format!("{}{:>2}. {heading} ({} lines)\n", "   ".repeat(level - 1), n + 1, end - start));
    }
    out
}

/// `guide <card>` for a card over [`FULL_LINES`]: its title, then how to read
/// more, then its opening cut at a paragraph boundary, then the numbered
/// section index (and the ways to read more again). `None` when the card
/// prints whole.
// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — the run-on hint sits under the title, so a short opening never reads as the whole card.
pub fn overview(name: &str, card: &str) -> Option<String> { overview_with(name, card, true) }

/// The same overview with the card's first section printed between the
/// opening and the index: what `guide <card>` prints when stdout is not a
/// terminal (an agent's tool), so one call shows the card's first content
/// and not only its table of contents. A first section longer than
/// [`FIRST_SECTION_LINES`] is cut at a paragraph boundary with the command
/// that prints the rest.
// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — 11 lanes re-ran --section/--full 3–4 times after an index-only opening.
pub fn overview_and_first_section(name: &str, card: &str) -> Option<String> { overview_with(name, card, true) }

/// The most lines of a card's first section the piped overview prints.
const FIRST_SECTION_LINES: usize = 80;

/// Tool reads use the same bounded discovery view as terminal reads.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000.000#8 — hint before the body survives batched tool truncation.
pub fn piped(name: &str, card: &str) -> String {
    if name == "start" { return bounded_default(name, card); }
    bounded_default(name, &overview_and_first_section(name, card).unwrap_or_else(|| card.to_owned()))
}

/// The last paragraph boundary (a blank line outside a fence) in
/// `lines[from..to]` at or before `limit`, as an absolute line number.
fn boundary(lines: &[&str], from: usize, to: usize, limit: usize) -> Option<usize> {
    let mut fenced = false;
    let mut best = None;
    let mut content = false;
    for (i, line) in lines.iter().enumerate().take(to).skip(from) {
        if line.trim_start().starts_with("```") { fenced = !fenced; }
        else if !fenced && content && line.trim().is_empty() && i <= limit { best = Some(i); }
        if !line.trim().is_empty() { content = true; }
    }
    best
}

fn overview_with(name: &str, card: &str, first_section: bool) -> Option<String> {
    let total = card.lines().count();
    let found = sections(card);
    if total <= FULL_LINES && card.len() <= FULL_BYTES { return None; }
    let lines: Vec<&str> = card.lines().collect();
    if found.is_empty() {
        // A single long fenced recipe is indivisible. Show its prose opening,
        // never half of a runnable code fence; --full prints the entire recipe.
    for &i in &hits {
        let (start, end) = (i.saturating_sub(1), (i + 2).min(lines.len()));
        match ranges.last_mut() { Some(last) if start <= last.1 => last.1 = end, _ => ranges.push((start, end)) }
    }
    let mut out = if any {
        format!("no line of guide {name} mentions all of {words:?}; {} {} one of them (a|b searches for either; words with spaces must share a line)\n", hits.len(), if hits.len() == 1 { "line mentions" } else { "lines mention" })
    } else { format!("guide {name}: {} {} {words:?}\n", hits.len(), if hits.len() == 1 { "line mentions" } else { "lines mention" }) };
    let (mut printed, mut shown, mut current) = (0, 0, usize::MAX);
    for (start, end) in ranges {
        if printed >= FIND_LINES { break; }
        let (n, heading) = section_of(&found, start.max(hits.iter().copied().find(|&h| h >= start).unwrap_or(start)));
        if n != current { out.push_str(&format!("{n}. {heading}\n")); current = n; } else { out.push_str("  …\n"); }
        for (i, line) in lines.iter().enumerate().take(end).skip(start) {
            let hit = hits.binary_search(&i).is_ok();
            out.push_str(&format!("{:>5}{} {line}\n", i + 1, if hit { ':' } else { ' ' }));
            printed += 1;
            shown += usize::from(hit);
        }
    }
    if shown < hits.len() { out.push_str(&format!("… {} more; narrow the words or read one section with --section <number>\n", hits.len() - shown)); }
    out.push_str(&format!("Whole sections: snapback4 guide {name} --section <number>"));
    if any { Err(out) } else { Ok(out) }
}


/// Paragraphs and complete fences are the units of reading and project deltas.
// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#8 — never cut a runnable fence in half.
pub fn reading_blocks(text: &str) -> Vec<String> {
    let mut blocks = Vec::new(); let mut block = String::new(); let mut fenced = false;
    for line in text.lines() {
        if line.trim_start().starts_with("```") { fenced = !fenced; }
        if line.is_empty() && !fenced {
            if !block.is_empty() { blocks.push(std::mem::take(&mut block)); }
        } else { block.push_str(line); block.push('\n'); }
    }
    if !block.is_empty() { blocks.push(block); } blocks
}

/// Conservative byte-token ceiling: any UTF-8 byte needs at most one byte token.
pub fn bounded_default(name: &str, text: &str) -> String {
    if text.len() <= FULL_BYTES { return text.to_owned(); }
    let hint = format!("\nTruncated: default budget is {FULL_BYTES} bytes. New question: snapback4 guide {name} --section <words>; section list: --full.\n");
    let mut out = String::new();
    for block in reading_blocks(text) {
        if out.len() + block.len() + hint.len() + 1 > FULL_BYTES { break; }
        out.push_str(&block); out.push('\n');
    }
    out.push_str(&hint); out
}
