## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
type Read<T> = { data: T; complete: boolean; fresh: boolean; since?: number; next?: Cursor }
            | { loading: true } | { denied: Refusal };
row.pending: boolean;
type Write = { state: "pending" } | { state: "sent"; seq: number } | { state: "failed"; why: Refusal };
type Link = "online" | "offline" | "signed-out";
# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A device per-query result cache.** No range receipts, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7). The server's narrow Postgres revision-equality cache and explicitly clock-qualified G6 read grant are the exceptions below; cross-revision promotion remains excluded.
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, one platform binary at a time. SQLite remains the default store; Postgres deploy/serve admits the initial Unix-socket HTTP profile under LLP 3000.009.001 (Moves, 2026-10-20; `guide release-json`). Provider HA, full parity and publication remain outside that profile.

## Moves

- **2026-10-24 — a bounded server read grant moves onto the doing-list** (author's G6 commission, LLP 3000.009.001.000.002 §4.2). It unblocks zero-flight Postgres hits after G5's measured validation CPU limit. Taken off the doing-list: mandatory per-hit database validation for the explicitly qualified, single-owner Linux deployment. Unqualified clocks/failover and lost/uncertain epochs use G5; device result caches and cross-revision promotion remain excluded.

- **2026-10-24 — Postgres server revision-equality read reuse** (LLP
  3000.009.001.000.002 §4/§6 G5). This removes repeated interpretation and
  data decoding for eligible pure queries at an unchanged durable revision.
  Taken off: the blanket server result-cache exclusion. Every reuse still
  validates fresh authority, revision and code in one database statement;
  SQLite stays on ordinary reads because it has no complete revision stream.
  Device caches, dependency promotion, retained authority and zero-flight
  grants stay excluded. Operator-local counters add no HTTP or authoring API.

- **2026-10-20 — Snapback 4 on Postgres moves onto the doing-list** (author: "commit and push these things, then do your \"what i'd do next\" things, then get this working on postgres and measure it"; LLP 3000.009.001 and its children). Unblocks: the store-port extraction on SQLite (P0, including the `Committer` seam with the serial batch); the Postgres adapters for every durable-state family and `serve`/`deploy`/`doctor` over Postgres (P1), with store-held fencing, the declared HA mode and `BlobStore`; the optimistic committer, commissioned on 2026-10-19 and built after its execution stage is sized; the scale program of LLP 3000.009.001.002 with its raw-Postgres, Supabase and Convex controls on fleet machines; and a research note comparing replicated SQLite with Postgres for applications that outgrow one SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation-only framing of the Postgres adapter. Stands: no publication or managed-service deployment authority, no lowering of Quarry to SQL, no SQL writes to managed data, one core owner. Several serving processes (P2), log retention, live graduation (LLP 3000.009.001.001) and the async-safe external anchor each wait for their own Moves line.

- **2026-10-13 — stored lower lifetimes (`not before`) are commissioned by PAGE2** (LLP 3000.036.003). This unblocks timed disclosure without an application publication job. Per-application jobs that flip a publication flag leave this admitted path; clock expressions in rules, captured lower lifetimes for read-through children, and boundary recounts for maintained data remain excluded. HS owns handshake transport; PAGE2 declares the new semantic capability.

- **2026-10-06 — exact Following history (FH delegated round-1 ruling).**
  Admit a Following-specific server traversal and bounded volatile frontier/replay
  state under LLP 3000.026 §9. The approved design reads exact history online
  from the first page; the SDK will own continuation. The first milestone is internal event metadata
  and the oracle, with no public completeness claim. Taken off: a blanket ban on
  state for this one expiring read. General result caches, resumable queries,
  device traversal, inbox coverage certificates and history-only blocks remain
  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.

- **2026-09-26 — server operation latency (LLP 3000.019 §3.7).** Calibration #22
  measured each read once and could not claim repeated p95/degradation testing.
  Unblocks percentile measurements of the application's own load without a
  custom timing harness. Taken off: a latency harness written by the builder;
  general metrics, tracing infrastructure and an admin console remain excluded.

- **2026-09-23 — Quarry states intent moves onto the doing-list** (Charlie:
  "if you need to try making *significant* architectural reworks in order to
  make the data modeling, queries, and scalability be excellent and elegant,
  you can experiment with that"; LLP 3000.013). Unblocks reads that state
  predicates and order, one `view` declaration for derived data, Following
  without caps, jobs and aggregates without author-chosen bounds, an app
  object and an offline web shell. Taken off: index names and author-chosen
  engine numbers on the paved path; the timeline's promotion, pull routes and
  caps; the "Time in rules" refusal is unchanged. Stands: one interpreter, no
  JavaScript in the data plane, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no relevance-ranked
  search.

- **2026-09-16 — the remaining general-purpose program moves onto the doing-list**
  (Charlie: "complete everything ... we'll remove services when we are done
  with the workroom test rather than wait to add them in ... use the fleet";
  LLP 3000.009 §14.7). Unblocks the §7 integrated-service baseline, C3 production
  Postgres, D's authorized database capabilities and measurement-led C2/E
  work while Workroom continues. Taken off: waiting for Workroom before
  starting services, and keeping production Postgres integration outside the
  current implementation program. The completed Workroom record drives a
  retain/remove pass. Stands: one interpreter and managed writer, no managed
  SQL writes, no JavaScript transactions, no SQL in the first Workroom cell,
  no invented performance/real-provider evidence, no expo-build-1000 load,
  and no new publication or managed-service deployment authority.

- **2026-09-16 — application SQL reads move onto the doing-list** (Charlie: "admit application SQL reads"; LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4, increment D). Unblocks reporting and search over authorized projection inputs and declared database-owned tables, with authorization before selection, ordering and limits. Taken off in the same change: the inspection-only exclusion for application SQL and D's pending-author gate. Implementation and qualification remain pending; SQLite and Postgres halves ship together, channels follow candidates, and A1–A3 plus the fixture pack stay first. Stands: no SQL writes into managed data, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no SQL in the first Snapback brief cell, and no publication authorization.

- **2026-09-15 — a Postgres evaluation backend moves onto the doing-list** (author: "Build the evaluation prototype"). Unblocks concrete evaluation of storage agnosticism, interpreter/database round trips and transactional sync ordering. This bounded prototype replaces full CLI/deployment integration as the next backend task; those product surfaces remain off this increment's doing-list. It shares Quarry, rules, maintains and partition logic, but uses an experimental Postgres store. Native SQL exists only as an explicitly separate comparison workload, not an application escape hatch. SQLite remains the production and local-device store. See [the experiment](./experiments/postgres/README.md).

- **2026-09-15 — the server half of LLP 3000.009 moves onto the doing-list** (author: "begin implementation using astra xhigh to code"; LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increments A1–A3). Unblocks the online-only brief's invitation, webhook and scheduled journeys without a second application server: work records whose authority is bound at admission, ids bound to their operation and arguments, declared action results, a generated handler context with a durable record per external step, declared routes and webhooks, scheduled actions under service seats, and `serve` supervising the runtime. Taken off in the same change: "A jobs framework" narrows — actions, effects and scheduled actions get per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation; priorities, worker pools and dead letters stay out — and the actions card's "supervise the worker as an ordinary Node process" leaves. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no identity provider in the engine, and no `ctx.sql`, projections or SQL reads in this increment.

- **2026-09-15 — the Postgres access boundary moves onto the doing-list** (LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increment C1). Unblocks measuring Postgres behind a batched access boundary instead of row-at-a-time calls: transaction-scoped prefetch, a pipelined commit tail, one portable index-key bound checked on both stores, and the corpus, conformance and device suites on both stores. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation prototype's single-adapter framing, in which the only next step was an unbatched store. Stands: Postgres is not a hosted or deployable target; its refusals of assets, jobs, actions and schedules, and `serve`, `deploy` and backup on Postgres, wait for increment C3.

- **2026-09-13 — word search, effects, service seats and presence move onto the doing-list** (author: "add all the things we need for Clique and Interview"; the full-brief Clique record's remaining capability lines and Interview's adapter responsibilities). What it unblocks: Clique M5's three search scopes online and offline on one index, M6's push seam without connection leases hand-built in tables, M8's digest emitted rather than logged into a table, Interview's phone sign-in and model workers as a service that mints sessions instead of proxying every request, and the ranked feed of LLP 3000.004 on the `feeds` card. Taken off in the same change: "an embedded JS engine for effects, until measured" (the engine never calls out; a worker does), "word search" from the package's exclusions. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no relevance ranking, no identity provider in the engine.
- **2026-09-12 — expiry, assets and jobs move onto the doing-list** (author: "complete snapback4 to the point where the entire clique benchmark can be run"). What it unblocks: the struck half of the Clique brief (M4 media, M5 view-once and expiry with no client open, M8's scheduled digest). Taken off in the same change: the "one photo path" and "cron syntax" exclusions above narrow to transcoding and to cron beyond `every N [at HH:MM]`; the "time in rules" exclusion stands, answered by `expire at .col` rather than a rule.
- **2026-09-13 — native jobs on (author: "write up an LLP about this and then implement").** Unblocks the scoring half of a feed — floats, vectors, real loops, a typed schema — without putting any of it into Quarry, and gives the model half of LLP 3000.004 a seat inside the bounds: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings, fuel, no I/O. Off the doing-list in the same change: the "external ranker with a service seat" of LLP 3000.004 §6.5 is no longer the only shape for compute over the store; it remains the shape for the network. Ranked and vector search, effects execution and an embedded JS engine stay above.
- **2026-09-13 — `sql`, a fifth named observability verb, moves onto the doing-list** (author ruling on LLP 3000.007 §14.10). Read-only SQL over generated per-table views of the developer's own store, under `dev` or against a `--release` directory; never rule-filtered, never an application surface. What it unblocks: the ground truth two Clique essays said Snapback lacked beside Postgres ("there is no underlying Postgres to query"), and the inspection the 0.2.6 Astra lane did by copying its SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: SQL reads as an application query surface (LLP 3000.007 §9.1) leave the doing-list until the reporting gap is measured after the language relaxations; reporting is Quarry's, inspection is `sql`'s. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, one authoring language.

- **2026-09-14 — the simulator witness is published for app builders (LLP 3000.007 §20.10, S30).** Unblocks native offline proof artifacts; replaces the repository-only Metro witness on the doing-list. Real-browser and device witnesses still cover only what the deterministic simulator cannot reach.

- **2026-09-14 — development clock control (S27, calibration F5)** moves onto the doing-list to unblock expiry and scheduled-digest tests without real-minute waits or backdated seeds; waiting on real time leaves the development test path, while production clock controls stay off the doing-list.

- **2026-09-25 — ephemeral tables enact the standing realtime line of LLP 3000.009 §12 (LLP 3000.017).** Unblocks typing and viewing without durable outbox traffic. Nothing leaves the doing-list: this enacts an already admitted scope. Last-seen storage, multi-server presence, a separate presence stream and ephemeral authority over durable invariants remain excluded.

- **2026-09-30 — bounded client subscription inspection (author's PR round-0 task)**
  moves onto the doing-list: query-group queue/in-flight/withdrawal counts and
  bounded latency/cause samples explain mounted screens flashing empty. Taken off
  the doing-list: builders must reconstruct this pressure from ad-hoc console
  instrumentation. General observability beyond the named verbs remains excluded;
  there is no telemetry transport or server admin console (LLP 3000.023 §10).
1:# Rules
12:## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit
14:- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
15:- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
18:- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
19:- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
20:- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
25:## Blocking checks
29:## Loop shape
31:- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
32:- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
34:- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**
36:## Time budgets
50:## Scope
52:- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
54:- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
55:- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
57:- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
58:- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
61:## Agents
63:- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
65:- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
69:- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
70:- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
73:## Shared machines
75:- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
76:- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
77:- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

# Rename a field or an enum value, end to end

A field rename preserves the same stored cell under a new name. Enum spelling
aliases carry values through release switches with reversible migration receipts.

```js
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { mkdtemp, mkdir, writeFile, rm } from "node:fs/promises";
import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { deploy, rollback, startServe, cli } from "snapback4/proof";
const root = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), "snapback4-rename-"));
const cwd = process.cwd();
let host;
try {
  process.chdir(root);
  const old = `table tickets:
  label: text <=80
  state: enum('queued', 'done')
  public 'rename example'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  update <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  online only
mutation add(label: text <=80):
  return insert tickets {label, state:'done'}
mutation edit(id: tickets, label: text <=80):
  return update tickets[id] {label, state:'queued'}
`;
  const current = old.replace("label: text <=80\n", "title: text <=80 renamed from label\n")
    .replaceAll("'done'", "'ready'")
    .replaceAll("label: text <=80)", "title: text <=80 alias label)")
    .replaceAll("{label,", "{title,") + "alias tickets.state 'done' = 'ready'\n";
  for (const [name, source] of [["v1", old], ["v2", current]]) {
    await mkdir(`versions/${name}`, { recursive:true });
    await writeFile(`versions/${name}/schema.q`, source);
  }
  const directory = join(root, "production");
  await deploy(directory, "versions/v1");
  host = await startServe(directory);
  const token = await host.operatorToken();
  async function write(op, args) {
    const response = await fetch(`${host.url}/m/${op}`, { method:"POST", headers:{"content-type":"application/json", authorization:`Bearer ${token}`}, body:JSON.stringify({args}), signal:AbortSignal.timeout(10000) });
    assert.equal(response.status, 200);
    const result = await response.json(); assert.equal(result.state, "sent"); return result.result;
  }
  async function rows() {
    const result = await cli(["data", "tickets", "--release", directory, "--json"]);
    assert.equal(result.code, 0, result.stderr);
    const page = JSON.parse(result.stdout); assert.equal(page.more, false);
    return new Map(page.rows.map(row => [row.id, row]));
  }
  const historical = await write("add", {label:"original"});
  await deploy(directory, "versions/v2");
  assert.equal((await rows()).get(historical.id).state, "ready");
  await write("edit", {id:historical.id, label:"edited"}); // old wire name still works
  const native = await write("add", {title:"new in v2"});
  for (let cycle = 0; cycle < 2; cycle++) {
    await rollback(directory, 1);
    const older = await rows();
    assert.equal(older.get(historical.id).label, "edited");
    assert.equal(older.get(historical.id).state, "queued");
    assert.equal(older.get(native.id).state, "done");
    await rollback(directory, "latest");
    const newer = await rows();
    assert.equal(newer.get(historical.id).title, "edited");
    assert.equal(newer.get(native.id).title, "new in v2");
    assert.equal(newer.get(native.id).state, "ready");
  }
  console.log("rename: historical and v2-native rows survive two cycles");
} finally {
  await host?.stop(); process.chdir(cwd);
  await rm(root, {recursive:true, force:true});
}
```

For a changed computation, retain its adopted backfill name and definition.
If a conflict reports competing values: keep a bridge that represents the
current data, repair through an authorized mutation, use a new name for new
meaning, preview both directions, then select the destination. Pruning cannot
reset a migration name. `rollback --check --bridge <new-dir>` writes source and
a checklist even when exact reverse extraction refuses several candidate children.

Use `title: text <=80 renamed from label`
for a scalar field, or `parent: orders renamed from order on delete cascade`
for a reference; the selected release supplies its delete policy and preserves

fn launcher_port(dir: &std::path::Path, name: &str) -> Option<u16> {
    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(dir.join("scripts/launch.mjs")).ok()?;
    // Recognize the generated fallback expression, not client application code.
    let marker = if name == "backend" { "low === null ? " } else { ": low === null ? " };
    text.split_once(marker)?.1.split_whitespace().next()?.parse().ok()
}

pub(super) fn apply(dir: &std::path::Path, additions: &[(&str, String)]) -> std::io::Result<()> {
    for (name, body) in additions {
        let path = dir.join(name);
        std::fs::create_dir_all(path.parent().unwrap())?;
        if *name == "package.json" || *name == "vite.config.mts" || *name == "scripts/frontend-proof.mjs" || *name == "scripts/day2-proof.mjs" { super::replace_file(&path, body)?; }
        else {
            use std::io::Write;
            let mut file = std::fs::OpenOptions::new().write(true).create_new(true).open(&path)?;
            file.write_all(body.as_bytes())?;
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Environment is evaluated whenever Vite loads the config, never at init time.
pub(super) fn runtime_vite(text: &str, backend: u16, frontend: u16) -> String {
    if text.contains("const proofRange") { return text.to_owned(); }
    let header = format!(r#"const proofRange = [process.env.PORT_LO, process.env.PORT_HI];
if (proofRange.some(v => v !== undefined) && (!proofRange.every(v => v && /^\d+$/.test(v)) || Number(proofRange[0]) < 1 || Number(proofRange[1]) > 65535 || Number(proofRange[0]) >= Number(proofRange[1]))) throw Error("PORT_LO/PORT_HI must provide at least two valid ports");
const backendPort = proofRange[0] === undefined ? {backend} : Number(proofRange[0]);
const frontendPort = proofRange[0] === undefined ? {frontend} : backendPort + 1;
            for (i, _) in columns.iter().enumerate() {
                values.push(match row.get_ref(i).map_err(query_error)? {
                    ValueRef::Null => Value::Null,
                    ValueRef::Integer(value) if kinds[i] == InspectionColumnKind::Bool => json!(value != 0),
                    ValueRef::Integer(value) => json!(value),
                    ValueRef::Real(value) => json!(value),
                    ValueRef::Text(value) if kinds[i] == InspectionColumnKind::Media => {
                        let value: crate::Value = serde_json::from_slice(value)
                            .map_err(|error| ("E_STORE", format!("stored media value in {} is invalid: {error}", columns[i])))?;
                        crate::wire::to_json(&value)
                    }
                    ValueRef::Text(value) => json!(String::from_utf8_lossy(value)),
                    ValueRef::Blob(value) => {
                        json!(value.iter().map(|b| format!("{b:02x}")).collect::<String>())
                    }
                });
            }
            rows.push(values);
        }
        let more = rows.len() > limit as usize;
        if more {
            rows.pop();
        }
        Ok(InspectionRows {
            columns,
            rows,
            more,
        })
    }

# Production proofs

Prove production using your existing `prod:serve` or `probe:serve` script. The kit checks its lifetime and cleanup:

```sh
npx snapback4 serve-check --deployment production --script prod:serve --up-ms 30000 --then 'npm run day2:business'
```

It checks one JSON readiness line, keeps the script up for at least 30 seconds
including business work, sends group SIGTERM and checks shutdown/store release.
The business command reads `SNAPBACK_URL`, `FRONTEND_URL` and private
`SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON` in memory. Never print credentials. No custom serve checker
is needed. Keep release moves awaited inside that command.

## Serve script and reusable accounts

Deploy once with `snapback4 deploy production`; build the frontend with its
`build` (web) or `build:web` (Expo) script. Save as scripts/prod-serve.mjs and
point package.json `prod:serve` at `node scripts/prod-serve.mjs`. This example
uses the operator-only `provision` mutation from `guide staging`; replace its
handle, roles and `args(account)` with your application's actual enrollment plan.

<!-- DX15 PRODUCTION SERVE BEGIN -->
```js
import { startServe, startStatic, serveUntilSignal, cleanup } from "snapback4/proof";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api.ts";
const backend = await startServe("production", {
  provisionUsers: {roles: {admin: 1, member: 1}, provision: api.provision},
});
let frontend;
try {
  frontend = await startStatic("dist");
  console.log(JSON.stringify({backendUrl: backend.url, frontendUrl: frontend.url,
    accounts: backend.sessions})); // private readiness, consumed only by serve-check
  await serveUntilSignal([backend, frontend]);
} finally {
  await cleanup(() => frontend?.stop(), () => backend.stop());
}
```
<!-- DX15 PRODUCTION SERVE END -->

`serveUntilSignal` holds a referenced lifetime until SIGTERM/SIGINT and awaits
host cleanup. Emitting readiness and returning is insufficient. `startStatic`
serves the built SPA with its fallback; build with the deployed backend URL
configuration your client uses.

Reruns log in to the same accounts for fresh sessions and provision unfinished
entries only. `backend.sessions.admin[0]` (and member[0]) is a real user account:
`{kind:"user", role, email, password, principal, token, expiresAt}`. Signup grants
no role; the ordinary operator mutation enrolls each account. Custom fixed
credentials should login first, then signup only if necessary; inspect `.why`
on `{ok:false}`. An ambiguous signup may already have created the account:
try login with those credentials before requesting another account. Auth
requests retain production rate budgets: reuse sessions within a run.

The private file `production/proof-accounts/default.json` (0600) holds
`{version:1, plan: string, entries:[{role,index,email,password,id,done,principal?,args?}]}`.
`plan` is a serialized object `{roles:[[role,count]],provision,service,users?}`;
`id` is the stable provision intent ID, `args` its canonical arguments, `done`
marks completed setup. These are credentials, not source files. Keep the roster
with its store across copies/backups; do not delete it to rerun. `roster:"name"`
intentionally selects another batch. A plan mismatch names supplied and pinned
plans and refuses before auth requests; keep the pinned plan or deliberately
choose a new name. Changed args, revoked credentials or partial setup need
operator inspection. A live `.lock` refuses concurrent setup.

## Admin inspection

`data roles --release production --all --json` reads every active row in one
read snapshot, bypassing application rules. The default remains 50 rows; a
truncated result points directly to `--all`. In a proof use
`await snapshotAll("roles", {release:"production"})` from `snapback4/proof`.
Ephemeral rows have no on-disk snapshot. For an application query, exhaust
`read.next` with `exhaustPages(c => client.query(api.list, {c}))`; completeness
and cursor exhaustion are separate. A complete empty list is `absent`, so
`matchRead` must handle `absent: () => "No rows"` (`guide reads`).

## A persona demo becomes an account app

Bare `use identity` keeps guests; guests call `/auth/guest` before any read;
`use identity password` removes them. `/auth/config` is GET; production needs
persisted sessions and trusted first writers because signup grants no role.

```quarry
use identity password
table labels:
  slug: text 1..40
  unique slug
  cap 100
  immutable slug

codex

I’m also checking section splitting for unreachable continuation content.

/// A card's `##`/`###` headings outside code fences: (level, heading, first line, end line).
pub fn sections(card: &str) -> Vec<(usize, String, usize, usize)> {
    let lines: Vec<&str> = card.lines().collect();
    let mut fenced = false;
    let mut found: Vec<(usize, String, usize, usize)> = Vec::new();
    for (index, line) in lines.iter().enumerate() {
        if line.trim_start().starts_with("```") { fenced = !fenced; continue; }
        if fenced { continue; }
        let level = line.chars().take_while(|c| *c == '#').count();
        if !(2..=3).contains(&level) || !line[level..].starts_with(' ') { continue; }
        found.push((level, line[level..].trim().to_owned(), index, lines.len()));
    }
    // A section runs to the next heading at its level or above.
    for i in 0..found.len() {
        let level = found[i].0;
        if let Some(next) = found[i + 1..].iter().find(|next| next.0 <= level) { found[i].3 = next.2; }
    }
    found
}

/// the card's opening and a numbered section index: agents batch several
/// cards per command and their tools truncate long output (the start card,
/// 124 lines at most, stays whole).
pub const FULL_LINES: usize = 240;
/// Dense cards with very long lines need the same bounded first read.
pub const FULL_BYTES: usize = 8_000;

     1	//! A day-one headless client must keep its own handles and module boundary.
     2	// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#9 — capture before editing; never overwrite historical evidence.
     3	use std::{path::Path, io};
     4	use serde_json::json;
     5	
     6	pub(super) fn files(dir: &Path, sources: &[(String, String)]) -> io::Result<Vec<(&'static str, String)>> {
     7	    if dir.join("v1").exists() { return Ok(Vec::new()); }
     8	    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(sources);
     9	    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() { return Err(io::Error::other("cannot freeze an unchecked v1 client; fix current schema first")); }
    10	    let mut files = Vec::new();
    11	    fn push(files: &mut Vec<(&'static str,String)>, path: String, text: String) {
    12	        files.push((Box::leak(path.into_boxed_str()),text));
    13	    }
    14	    fn walk(root: &Path, relative: &Path, files: &mut Vec<(&'static str,String)>) -> io::Result<()> {
    15	        let path = root.join(relative);
    16	        if !path.exists() { return Ok(()); }
    17	        if path.symlink_metadata()?.file_type().is_symlink() { return Err(io::Error::other(format!("frozen client refuses symlink {}", path.display()))); }
    18	        if path.is_dir() {
    19	            for entry in std::fs::read_dir(path)? { walk(root, &relative.join(entry?.file_name()),files)?; }
    20	        } else if matches!(path.extension().and_then(|s|s.to_str()), Some("mjs"|"cjs"|"js"|"ts"|"tsx"|"jsx")) {
    21	            push(files,format!("v1/{}",relative.display()),std::fs::read_to_string(path)?);
    22	        }
    23	        Ok(())
    24	    }
    25	    // Save application modules, not node_modules, stores, credentials or build output.
    26	    for path in ["scripts", "src", "app"] { walk(dir,Path::new(path),&mut files)?; }
    27	    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(dir)? {
    28	        let entry=entry?;
    29	        if entry.path().is_file() && entry.path().extension().is_some_and(|e|matches!(e.to_str(),Some("mjs"|"js"|"ts"|"tsx"))) { walk(dir,Path::new(&entry.file_name()),&mut files)?; }
    30	    }
    31	    for (name,text) in sources { push(&mut files,format!("v1/snapback/{name}"),text.clone()); }
    32	    push(&mut files,"v1/snapback/generated/api.ts".into(),snapback4_lang::emit::typescript(&bound));
    33	    push(&mut files,"v1/snapback/generated/package.json".into(),"{\"type\":\"module\"}\n".into());
    34	    let package: serde_json::Value = std::fs::read(dir.join("package.json")).ok().and_then(|b|serde_json::from_slice(&b).ok()).unwrap_or_default();
    35	    let command=package["scripts"]["client:calls"].as_str();
    36	    let mut frozen=json!({"private":true,"type":"module","scripts":{"client:calls":"node .snapback4-frozen-run.mjs"}});
    37	    if package["scripts"].is_object() { frozen["scripts"]=package["scripts"].clone(); }
    38	    frozen["scripts"]["client:calls"]=json!("node .snapback4-frozen-run.mjs");
    39	    if let Some(command)=command { frozen["scripts"]["client:calls:frozen"]=json!(command); }
    40	    for group in ["dependencies","devDependencies"] { if package[group].is_object() { frozen[group]=package[group].clone(); } }
    41	    push(&mut files,"v1/package.json".into(),super::pretty(&frozen));
    42	    push(&mut files,"v1/.snapback4-frozen-run.mjs".into(),include_str!("frozen-run.mjs").into());
    43	    Ok(files)
    44	}
     1	// API signatures: snapback4 guide api proof | client | auth | generated
     2	// Frozen source uses its saved API. Inline users are adapted for legacy file readers.
     3	import { mkdtemp, writeFile, rm, readFile } from "node:fs/promises";
     4	import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
     5	import { join, dirname } from "node:path";
     6	import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
     7	import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
     8	const cwd = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
     9	const pkg = JSON.parse(await readFile(join(cwd, "package.json"), "utf8"));
    10	if (!pkg.scripts?.["client:calls:frozen"]) throw Error("No day-one client:calls was present at capture; add a business client before claiming frozen-client coverage.");
    11	let temporary;
    12	const env = { ...process.env };
    13	try {
    14	  if (env.CALLS_USERS?.trimStart().startsWith("{")) {
    15	    const users = JSON.parse(env.CALLS_USERS);
    16	    if (!users || Array.isArray(users) || Object.values(users).some(batch => !Array.isArray(batch))) throw Error("CALLS_USERS must be a role-to-account-arrays object");
    17	    temporary = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), "snapback4-v1-users-"));
    18	    env.CALLS_USERS = join(temporary, "users.json");
    19	    await writeFile(env.CALLS_USERS, JSON.stringify(users), {mode:0o600});
    20	  }
    21	  const child = spawn("npm", ["run", "--silent", "client:calls:frozen"], {cwd,env,stdio:"inherit"});
    22	  const signals = ["SIGTERM", "SIGINT"];
    23	  const forward = signal => { try { child.kill(signal); } catch {} };
    24	  const handlers = signals.map(signal => {const fn=()=>forward(signal);process.on(signal,fn);return fn;});
    25	  try {
    26	    const result = await new Promise((resolve,reject)=>{child.once("error",reject);child.once("exit",(code,signal)=>resolve({code,signal}));});
    27	    process.exitCode = result.code ?? 1;
    28	  } finally { signals.forEach((signal,i)=>process.off(signal,handlers[i])); }
    29	} finally { if (temporary) await rm(temporary,{recursive:true,force:true}); }
     1	//! Complete operator inspection over the existing read-only retained views.
     2	// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#9 — retained cells supplement active images once per id.
     3	use super::Options;
     4	use snapback4_core::{inspection::{InspectionCatalog,check_output_names},schema::Schema,store_port::AnyInspection};
     5	use serde_json::{Value,json};
     6	use std::{collections::BTreeMap,path::Path};
     7	
     8	type Result<T> = std::result::Result<T,(&'static str,String)>;
     9	pub(super) fn run(path: &Path, table: &str, options: &Options) -> i32 {
    10	    match inspect(path,table) {
    11	        Ok(rows) => {
    12	            if options.json { println!("{}",json!({"rows":rows,"more":false,"limit":null,"inspection":"active and retained records, merged by id"})); }
    13	            else { for row in rows {println!("{row}");} }
    14	            0
    15	        }
    16	        Err((code,message)) => { eprintln!("{code}: {message}"); 1 }
    17	    }
    18	}
    19	fn inspect(path: &Path, table: &str) -> Result<Vec<Value>> {
    20	    // Restrict identifiers before constructing the inspection SELECT.
    21	    if !table.bytes().enumerate().all(|(i,b)|b.is_ascii_alphabetic() || i>0 && (b.is_ascii_digit() || b==b'_')) || table.is_empty() {
    22	        return Err(("E_INPUT","data --all takes one application table name".into()));
    23	    }
    24	    let conn=AnyInspection::open(path)?;
    25	    conn.begin_snapshot()?;
    26	    let (_,encoded)=conn.active_schema_record()?;
    27	    let active:Schema=serde_json::from_str(&encoded).map_err(|e|("E_SCHEMA",e.to_string()))?;
    28	    active.refuse_private(table).map_err(|e|("E_SCHEMA",e.to_string()))?;
    29	    let schema=conn.retained_schema(active.clone())?;
    30	    schema.refuse_private(table).map_err(|e|("E_SCHEMA",e.to_string()))?;
    31	    let definition=schema.table(table).ok_or_else(||("E_SCHEMA",format!("no table {table}")))?;
    32	    if definition.ephemeral.is_some() {return Err(("E_SCHEMA",format!("data {table}: ephemeral rows live only in serving memory; no disk snapshot")));}
    33	    conn.build_retained_views(&schema,false,true,|_|{})?;
    34	    let sql=format!("SELECT * FROM \"{table}\" ORDER BY id, CASE _snapback_state WHEN 'active' THEN 0 WHEN 'dormant-row' THEN 1 ELSE 2 END");
    35	    let records=conn.query(&sql,u32::MAX,true,
    36	        |message|snapback4_core::inspection::query_error(&schema,message),
    37	        |columns|check_output_names(columns,true),
    38	        |columns|super::sql::object_kinds(&sql,&schema,columns,true).map_err(|e|("E_INPUT",e)))?;
    39	    if records.more { return Err(("E_INPUT","data --all inspection exceeded its addressable row count; no complete snapshot".into())); }
    40	    let mut merged:BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Map<String,Value>>=BTreeMap::new();
    41	    for values in records.rows {
    42	        let row:serde_json::Map<String,Value>=records.columns.iter().cloned().zip(values).collect();
    43	        let id=row.get("id").and_then(Value::as_str).ok_or(("E_SCHEMA","retained record missing id".into()))?;
    44	        let provided:Vec<String>=serde_json::from_str(row.get("_snapback_columns").and_then(Value::as_str).unwrap_or("[]")).map_err(|e|("E_SCHEMA",e.to_string()))?;
    45	        let is_active=row.get("_snapback_state").and_then(Value::as_str)==Some("active");
    46	        let target=merged.entry(id.to_owned()).or_default();
    47	        for (key,value) in row.iter().filter(|(key,_)| !key.starts_with("_snapback_")) {
    48	            let live_field=is_active && active.table(table).is_some_and(|t|t.columns.contains_key(key));
    49	            if key=="id" || live_field || provided.contains(key) { target.entry(key.clone()).or_insert_with(||value.clone()); }
    50	        }
    51	    }
    52	    Ok(merged.into_values().map(Value::Object).collect())
    53	}

//! The Snapback 4 binary (LLP 3000 §5, week 2): one small program with the
//! loop verbs. `check` compiles the schema in milliseconds and writes the
//! client's types; `dev` serves it and re-adopts it on every save; `run`,
//! `test`, `why`, `guide`, `doctor`, `data` and `init` do what they say.

mod client_compatibility;
mod witness_socket;
mod listen_refusal;
mod verb_help;
mod cli_flags;
mod guide_find;
mod guide_seen;
mod guide_api;
mod device_handshake;
mod exact_device;
mod note_rank;
mod persona_principals;
mod assets;
mod host_limits;
mod auth;
mod native;
mod release_control;
mod release_requests;
mod release_files;
mod project;
mod serve;
mod dev_worker;
mod worker_graph;
mod dev_replay;
mod dev_clock;
mod dev_hosts;
mod dev_reset;
mod dev_clock_store;
mod lifecycle;
mod thread_priority;
mod verbs;
mod port_flag;
mod timeline_cost;
mod column_cost;
mod scale;
mod presentation;
mod service_identities;
mod sync_wire;
mod store_owner;
mod store_config;
mod supervisor;

use snapback4_lang::guide;
use std::process::ExitCode;


fn main() -> ExitCode {
    let _phase_trace=verbs::phase::Trace;
    // Before any thread: the wrapper's PID leaves the environment here.
    supervisor::adopt();
    snapback4_core::diagnostics::set_thread_start(thread_priority::background);
    // Keep this first: lifecycle tests distinguish a pre-main process-launch
    // stall from a server that reached its own bounded readiness window.
    if std::env::var_os("SNAPBACK4_TEST_MAIN_REACHED").is_some() {
        eprintln!("SNAPBACK4_TEST_MAIN_REACHED");
    }
    let args: Vec<String> = std::env::args().skip(1).collect();
    let verb = args.first().map(String::as_str).unwrap_or("");
    if let Err(error) = cli_flags::validate(&args) {
        eprintln!("{error}"); return ExitCode::from(2);
    }
    let inherited = match witness_socket::take(verb) {
        Ok(listener) => listener,
        Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); return ExitCode::from(1); }
    };
    if verb == "--internal-witness-process" { return ExitCode::from(verbs::witness::process_group::command(&args[1..])); }
    if verb == "--internal-worker-watchdog" { return ExitCode::from(dev_worker::process::watchdog(&args[1..])); }
    quiet_broken_pipe(verb);
    let options = verbs::parse(&args);
    if options.flags.contains_key("help") || matches!(verb, "--help" | "-h") || verb.is_empty() {
        // `snapback4 test --help` is about test; `snapback4 --help` lists every verb.
        println!("{}", verb_help::page(verb).unwrap_or_else(cli_flags::top_help));
        return ExitCode::SUCCESS;
    }
    if options.flags.contains_key("replay") && (verb != "dev" || options.flags.contains_key("release")) {
        eprintln!("{}", dev_replay::dev_only());
        return ExitCode::from(1);
    }
    if (verb == "serve" || options.flags.contains_key("release")) && options.flags.contains_key("now") {
        eprintln!("{}", dev_clock::release());
        return ExitCode::from(1);
    }
    // Release-directory inspection can create SQLite WAL bookkeeping too.
    // Admit its directory before any administrative reader opens the database.
    // Token and lifecycle consumers perform this same guard at their own entry.

exec

## Day-two proof with production accounts

Run this in a fresh `init --web` starter after installing its dependencies.
Save the block as scripts/day2-accounts.mjs and run `node scripts/day2-accounts.mjs`.
It copies the installed package's complete production recipe (teams and memberships),
compiles and builds that UI, then signs in through its production account form.
The production entry recipe is maintained on `guide production`.
Adapt the assertions to your own business model when replacing this example.

When adapting an existing app's `prod:serve` or `probe:serve`, run business
assertions with `serve-check --then 'npm run day2:business'` or
`withServeScript` (`guide testing --section Check an npm serve script`). Never
write a serve driver. Keep release moves awaited inside that command/callback;
read credentials from SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON without printing them. The complete
recipe below owns its disposable deployment and frontend directly.

The proof keeps one live serve process and one browser page through v1 → v2 →
rollback → forward. It tests real password accounts, a rendered durable write,
existing-row defaults, a widened title, a new membership, authorization refusals,
and retained rows at every transition. The wider title needs a registered current
guarded rollback view, which also keeps the added member note. Saved sources and
deployments belong to its private scratch directory. It changes the fresh starter's working schema and UI to the
production recipe; run it in a copy when preserving an existing application.
Stage comparisons key rows by stable ids and compare only fields shared by the
two releases. They exclude ids created by the frozen v1 client because those
are new test data, allow a v2-native id to be dormant during exact-v1 rollback,
and require that v2-native row again after forwarding.

// A release's renames resolve simultaneously against its immutable predecessor.
// Cache authored sources too: returning forward may register that same source anew.
const identitiesByRelease = new Map(), identitiesBySource = new Map(), declarationsByRelease = new Map();
let lastOrdinaryIdentity;
function tableIdentities(status, declarations, sourceKey) {
  if (identitiesByRelease.has(status.release)) return identitiesByRelease.get(status.release);
  let identity;
  if (status.live.exact === false) {
    const target = identitiesByRelease.get(status.live.source);
    const parent = identitiesByRelease.get(status.live.parent);
    assert.ok(target && parent, "view must name previously measured target and parent releases");
    const viewHeaders = new Map(declarations);
    const parentHeaders = declarationsByRelease.get(status.live.parent);
    const targetHeaders = declarationsByRelease.get(status.live.source);
    const matches = (headers, name, from) => headers.has(name) && headers.get(name) === from;
    // Complete-current views retain their parent's names, even when an old name
    // was reused for a new table. Alpha-adapted selective views carry the
    // predecessor headers instead; matching names alone cannot prove lineage.
    const completeCurrent = [...parentHeaders].every(([name,from]) => matches(viewHeaders,name,from));
    identity = new Map(declarations.map(([name,from]) => {
      const useParent = matches(parentHeaders,name,from) && (completeCurrent || !matches(targetHeaders,name,from));
      const origin = useParent ? parent.get(name) : matches(targetHeaders,name,from) ? target.get(name) : undefined;
      assert.ok(origin, `${name}: generated view has no measured table lineage`);
      return [name,origin];
    }));
  } else if (identitiesBySource.has(sourceKey)) identity = identitiesBySource.get(sourceKey);
  else {
    const predecessor = lastOrdinaryIdentity;
    const moved = new Set(declarations.filter(([,from]) => from).map(([,from]) => from));
    identity = new Map(declarations.map(([name,from]) => [name,
      !predecessor ? name : from ? predecessor.get(from) ?? `${name}@${status.release}` :
      !moved.has(name) && predecessor.has(name) ? predecessor.get(name) : `${name}@${status.release}`]));
    identitiesBySource.set(sourceKey, identity);
    lastOrdinaryIdentity = identity;
  }
  assert.equal(new Set(identity.values()).size, identity.size, "distinct retained tables must have distinct identities");
  identitiesByRelease.set(status.release, identity);
  declarationsByRelease.set(status.release, new Map(declarations));
  return identity;
}

//! Release row lifetimes whose present values cannot inhabit the selected type.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#91-per-row-narrowed-value-dormancy — hide a reference-closed population; never coerce values.
use super::*;
use crate::schema::{ColumnType, Constraint};

pub(super) fn present(conn: &Connection) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    Ok(conn.query_row(
        "SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_dormant_narrow_rows')",
        [],
        |r| r.get(0),
    )?)
}

fn incompatible(tx:&Tx<'_>,schema: &Schema, name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    incompatible_except(tx,schema,name,row,&[])
}
fn incompatible_except(tx:&Tx<'_>,schema: &Schema,name:&str,row:&Value,authority:&[String])->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    let table = &schema.tables[name];
    for (column,kind) in &table.columns {
        if table.engine_field(column) || table.maintained.contains_key(column) || authority.contains(column) {continue;}
        if let Ok(value)=row.field(column) {
            if incompatible_column(tx, table, name, column, kind, value)? { return Ok(true); }
        }
    }
    Ok(false)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#11-wave-11-factual-release-narration — the warning and activation share the value predicate, including ledger-backed assets.
pub(super) fn incompatible_column(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: &Table, name: &str, column: &str, kind: &ColumnType, value: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    if crate::engine::validate::validate_column(name, column, kind, Some(value)).is_err() { return Ok(true); }
    // @ref LLP 3000.038#1-the-type — asset facts belong to the ledger, including optional/list leaves.
    match super::activation::validate_assets(tx, kind, Some(value)) {
        Err(RuntimeError::Asset { code: "E_ASSET_TYPE" | "E_ASSET_TOO_LARGE", .. }) => return Ok(true),
        Err(error) => return Err(error),
        Ok(()) => (),
    }
    Ok(table.constraints.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Constraint::Text { field, min, format }
        if field == column && crate::engine::validate::validate_text_value(field,value,*min,*format).is_err())))
}

fn migratable(kind:&ColumnType)->bool {
    match kind {ColumnType::Optional(k)=>migratable(k),ColumnType::Image{..}|ColumnType::Video{..}|ColumnType::File{..}|ColumnType::List{..}|ColumnType::ListRange{..}|ColumnType::Ref{..}=>false,_=>true}
}

fn refs(kind: &ColumnType, value: &Value, out: &mut Vec<(String, String)>) {
    match (kind, value) {
        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), v) => refs(inner, v, out),
        (ColumnType::List { of, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, .. }, Value::Array(vs)) => {
            for v in vs {
                refs(of, v, out);
            }
        }
        (ColumnType::Ref { table }, Value::Id(id)) => out.push((table.clone(), id.clone())),
        _ => {}
    }
}

pub(super) fn transition(
    tx: &Tx<'_>,
    next: &Schema,
    previous: Option<&Schema>,
    now: i64,
    restored_images: bool,
    source_definitions: &BTreeMap<String, Table>,
    authority_sources: &BTreeMap<String, Table>,
) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    // Backfill sources already retired through the ordinary action closure,
    // before patching. Never re-test predecessor deadlines on transformed rows.
    if next.backfills.is_empty() && next.downfills.is_empty() && !super::backfills::has_evidence(tx)? {retire_sources(tx,next,previous,now)?;}
    let have_dormant: bool = tx.conn().query_row(
        "SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows)",
        [],
        |r| r.get(0),
    )?;
    let type_change = previous.is_some_and(|old| {
        next.tables.iter().any(|(name, t)| {
            old.table(name).is_some_and(|p| {
                t.columns
                    .iter()
                    .any(|(c, k)| p.columns.get(c).is_some_and(|old| old != k))
                    || t.constraints
                        .iter()
                        .filter(|c| matches!(c, Constraint::Text { .. }))
                        .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                        != p.constraints
                            .iter()
                            .filter(|c| matches!(c, Constraint::Text { .. }))
                            .collect::<Vec<_>>()
            })
        })
    });
    if !have_dormant && !type_change && !restored_images && tx.meta("activation_population")?.as_deref()!=Some("true") {
        return Ok(());
    }
    let mut newly_retained = 0u64;
    // Stream on the primary key. Do not retain a store-sized list of images.
    restore_images(tx,next,previous,now,source_definitions,authority_sources,false)?;
    tx.clear_activation_scan();
    // References to a hidden row hide their dependents, including memberships.
    loop {
        let mut removed = 0u64;
        for (name, table) in next
            .tables
            .iter()

                            super::dormant::delete(tx,t,id,name)?;
                        } else {
                            tx.delete(name,source,&row)?;
                        }
                        return Ok(());
                    }

                let source=super::backfills::native_source(previous.and_then(|s|s.table(name)).unwrap_or(table),table,&native);
                crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,source.as_ref());
                let mut causes=std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
                for (column,kind) in &table.columns {
                    if table.engine_field(column) || table.maintained.contains_key(column) {continue;}
                    if let Ok(value)=row.field(column) {if incompatible_column(tx,table,name,column,kind,value)? {
                        let value=match value {Value::Null=>"null".to_owned(),Value::Array(v)=>format!("a list of {} values",v.len()),_=>format!("a value outside {kind}")};
                        let remedy=if migratable(kind) {"declare a truthful scalar down projection or use a loosened bridge"} else {"computed down cannot project files, images, collections or references; use a loosened bridge"};
                        causes.insert(format!("{name}.{column} holds {value}, which does not fit {kind}; {remedy}"));
                    }}
                }

//! Column admission, maintained fields and authority/identity/delivery dependencies.
// @ref LLP 3000.007.001#2 — the binder and raw-schema admission share this walk.
use super::*;

pub const GROUP: &str = "$parent";

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct ColumnOwner {
    pub table: String,
    pub column: String,
}

impl ColumnOwner {
    pub fn label(&self) -> String { format!("{}.{}", self.table, self.column) }
    // Bounded labels also keep a first contribution independent of parent state.
    pub fn target(&self) -> String { format!("$column:{}", digest(&(&self.table, &self.column))) }
}

impl Schema {
    pub(crate) fn private_owner(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&ColumnOwner> {
        // Privacy also holds before admission: a duplicate ordinary declaration
        // must never shadow a private one in a raw schema.
        self.maintains.iter().filter(|m| m.target == name).find_map(|m| m.private.as_ref())
    }

    pub fn visible(&self, name: &str) -> bool { self.private_owner(name).is_none() && !self.is_recount_work(name) }

    pub fn visible_tables(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&String, &Table)> {
        self.tables.iter().filter(|(name, _)| self.visible(name))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007.001#3.2 — identity remains on Table, which image-retention compares.
    pub fn public_projection(&self) -> Self {
        let mut schema = self.clone();
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        { schema.backfills.clear(); schema.downfills.clear(); schema.table_renames.clear(); schema.asset_renames.clear(); schema.extractions.clear(); }
        // Inspection provenance is catalog side metadata, never device input.
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        { schema.authored_indexes = Default::default(); }
        schema.prepare_receipt_dependencies();
        schema.receipt_dependencies.retain(|name, _| self.visible(name));
        schema.source_reservations.retain(|name, _| self.visible(name));
        schema.tables.retain(|name, _| self.visible(name));
        schema.maintains.retain(|m| self.visible(&m.target));
        schema
    }

    pub fn refuse_private(&self, name: &str) -> Result<(), crate::error::RuntimeError> {
        if self.is_recount_work(name) { return Err(crate::error::RuntimeError::Structural {code:"E_VIEW_KEY",message:"recount state is engine-owned; read the row-keyed view instead (guide maintains)".into()}); }
        if let Some(owner) = self.private_owner(name) {
            return Err(crate::error::RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_MAINTAINED_COLUMN_PRIVATE",
                message: format!("{} is maintained by the engine; read the parent row or write the source rows (guide maintains)", owner.label()) });
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// A projected device schema deliberately has metadata without private maintains.
    /// Authoritative engines must have exactly one derivation for every owned field.
    pub fn validate_column_owners(&self) -> Result<(), crate::error::RuntimeError> {
        for (name,table) in self.visible_tables() {
            for column in table.maintained.keys() {
                if self.maintains.iter().filter(|m|m.private.as_ref().is_some_and(|o|&o.table == name && &o.column == column)).count() != 1 {
                    return Err(crate::error::RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{name}.{column} needs exactly one engine derivation; declare the column aggregate (guide maintains)")));
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

impl Maintain {
    pub fn group_fields(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        if self.private.is_some() { vec![GROUP.into()] } else { self.by.clone() }
    }

    pub fn derivation_identity(&self) -> String { digest(&(&self.kind, &self.source, &self.filter, &self.by)) }
    pub fn label(&self) -> String { self.private.as_ref().map_or_else(|| self.target.clone(), ColumnOwner::label) }
}

struct Walk<'a> { schema: &'a Schema, issues: Vec<SchemaIssue> }
impl Walk<'_> {
    fn issue(&mut self, path: &str, label: &str) {
        self.issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.into(), message: format!("maintained column {label} is depended on by {path}; use an ordinary column for authority, identity, lifetime or delivery, or a separate maintain for an aggregate (guide maintains)") });
    }
    fn target(&mut self, table: &str, path: &str) {
        if self.schema.is_recount_work(table) {self.issues.push(SchemaIssue {path:path.into(),message:"E_VIEW_KEY: recount state is engine-owned; read the row-keyed view instead (guide maintains)".into()});}
        if let Some(owner) = self.schema.private_owner(table) { self.issue(path, &owner.label()); }
    }
    fn field(&mut self, table: &str, field: &str, path: &str) {
        if let Some(message) = self.schema.row_view_predicate_field(table, field) {
            self.issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.into(), message });
        }
        if self.schema.table(table).is_some_and(|t| t.maintained.contains_key(field)) { self.issue(path, &format!("{table}.{field}")); }
    }
    fn term(&mut self, table: &str, term: &RuleTerm, path: &str) {
        if let RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::OldField(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) = term { self.field(table, field, path); }
    }
    fn index(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, path: &str) {
        self.target(table, path);
        if let Some(definition) = self.schema.table(table) && let Some(index) = definition.index(index) {

    /// A cap grouping by a `set null` reference does not count its null rows
    /// (LLP 3000.039 §2.2), so it proves no population bound.
    pub fn cap_exempts_null(&self, by: &[String]) -> bool {
        by.iter().any(|column| self.on_delete.get(column) == Some(&references::OnDelete::SetNull))
    }

    pub fn immutable_fields(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &str> {
        self.constraints.iter().flat_map(|constraint| match constraint {
            Constraint::Immutable { fields } => fields.iter().map(String::as_str).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
            _ => Vec::new(),
        })
    }

    /// Whether `row` is past its `expire` column at `now`: absent to every
    /// reader, pending the sweep. A null time never expires.
    pub fn expired(&self, row: &Value, now: i64) -> bool {
        self.deadline(row).is_some_and(|at| at <= now)
    }

    /// The index the expiry sweep walks: the first leading with the
    /// `expire` column, as the declaration requires.
    pub fn expiry_index(&self) -> Option<(&str, &Index)> {
        let column = self.expiry_column()?;
        self.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| {
                index.is_plain() && index.columns().first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
            }).map(|(name, index)| (name.as_str(), index))
